戰火影響料屬短期，無礙市場中期走勢
2026 年 4 月
美國對伊朗展開襲擊所引發的中東戰火延續至今，拖累 3 月截至 27 日的全球金融股市表現，
市場在多重變數交織下動能顯著受壓，主要股市指數單月報酬全盤皆墨。不過，在能源類股大漲的支
撐下，S&P 500 指數表現相對其他股市穩定。日本股市雖亦出現獲利了結壓力，但受惠於高市政府
「負責且積極的財政政策」 及日圓匯率弱勢有利出口 ， 今年以來 MSCI 日本指數的表現僅次於拉丁美
洲股市。另一方面，受油價高漲帶動通膨預期、外資持續撤出且經濟放緩所累，印度股市出現賣壓，

MSCI 印度指數不論在 3 月或是今年至今表現都是敬陪末座。
全球主要債市 3 月報酬亦全數下跌。在所有券種之中 ， 以新興當地債下跌 5.21% 表現最為疲弱 ，
除了油價引發市場對於進口原油的新興國家前景的擔憂外 ，高漲的美元指數也使得當地貨幣計價新興
債表現呈壓。 全球主權債同樣因為油價高漲推升通膨疑慮 ，投資人對於各國央行貨幣政策或因而轉為
更為鷹派高度警戒，使其表現差強人意。
自去年川普關稅「解放日」後美股跌至低點後開始走揚，各國股市與主要指數持續上揚，其中又
以金礦指數、南韓股市、費半指數、日本股市表現最佳。然而，自中東戰事 2/28 爆發後，以指數修
正幅度來看，跌幅最大的市場分別為金礦指數、南韓股市、日本股市。唯有費半指數則在 AI 需求暢
旺的加持下成為例外。由此可見，本波市場的回跌，基本上為「漲多跌多」的修正效應。
就基本面來看，2026 年美股企業獲利增長主軸仍在 AI， 由於2026 年仍在 AI 基礎建設期擴張間 ，
AI 終端需求仍是科技龍頭企業，故所帶動的獲利增長料不致受油價影響，今年單季盈餘增長可望維

持 2 位數水準。自中東戰事以來，彭博對於 MSCI 美國指數 Q1 及 Q2 的 EPS 預期不跌反漲，顯示市
場對於美國企業的獲利前景仍極具信心。同時，目前的資訊科技類股的預期 P/E 比與過去 10 年的均
值相較仍過有遭到低估之嫌，也預示科技類股相較於其他類股仍有進一步向上的動能。
再者，儘管近期美國公債殖利率因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目
標，投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策
預期變動影響較大，故在年期與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

美國股市：標普 500 企業盈餘增長加速，私募信貸贖回潮不致衝擊美國經濟
美國第四季 GDP 季增年率修正值 0.7%，較初值 1.4%有所調降，主要反映實質個人消費季比降
至 0.7%。第四季 GDP 數據整體仍支持美國經濟穩健。但第一季受通膨(關稅與伊朗危機)與高利率影
響，消費行為呈現「降級消費」特徵，非必需性支出收縮，經濟呈現倚賴「高收入族群支撐消費」的
K 型分化格局。由於中東戰火推高能源價格，美國民眾擔心生活成本壓力惡化，美國密西根大學3 月
消費者信心指數降至 55.5，較 2 月終值 56.6 下降 1.1，觸及今年以來最低紀錄，反映中東危機與生

活成本壓力削弱民眾樂觀情緒。
2 月非農就業人數淨減少 9.2 萬個，低於彭博預期的增加 1.3 萬，失業率躍升至 4.4%，失業率雖
短期上揚、但就長期角度仍在低檔。追究原因數據主要是受到罷工和天氣等短期因素干擾，導致醫療
健保、建築業、零售業和休閒酒店等產業表現不佳，但整體勞動市場狀態溫和，從請領就業金人數等
數據看來就業市場尚未出現顯著惡化跡象。市場先前擔憂 AI 取代人力，目前數據看來企業僅是招募
保守，後續可觀察勞動市場降溫是否反映在薪資成長，若薪資壓力下降有助於抑制服務業通膨，除直
接改善企業成本結構外，也將使聯準會擔憂的服務業通膨出現放緩。
預估第一季標普 500 企業餘年增仍可達到 11.3%，且在第二季顯著拉升至近兩成水準，科技板
塊受惠於 AI 仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸。大型科技巨擘透過AI 優化流程，開始對利潤率產生正
面影響。然而，此一效益目前高度集中於資本與技術優勢明顯的科技龍頭企業，中小企業與非科技產
業尚未顯著受益。因此，企業獲利將呈現明顯分化格局，而尚未受惠於 AI 的非科技業者，將會在未

來幾年逐步提高使用 AI 程度，傳統企業大規模導入 AI 模型與使用算力，將可成為 AI 龍頭下一波業
績成長動能。
近期美國私募信貸市場爆發贖回潮，包含貝萊德、阿波羅、藍鷹(Blue Owl)等巨頭均遭投資人要
求提款，甚至限制贖回以防止流動性枯竭。此波動盪主因投資人擔憂 AI 技術衝擊軟體業導致違約風
險上升，以及對高收益資產的獲利了結。研判此類事件雖造成金融股拉回，但引發總體系統性危機的
可能性極低。
目前標普 500 本益比估計在 22.84 倍、股價淨值比 4.95 倍，短期評價偏高，面對伊朗戰事的干
擾，預料股市震盪加劇，但若市場拉回，基於美國在 AI 產業具備絕對優勢，仍建議以長線角度布局。

歐洲股市：歐元區經濟表現穩健，央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度
歐元區第四季 GDP 季增率自初值的 0.3%下修至 0.2%，年增則下修至 1.2%，二者皆低於市場預
期。歐元區第四季經濟動能略微放緩，成長主要由內需帶動。家庭消費在穩健的勞動力市場與薪資增
長支撐下，季增率上升至 0.2%，固定資本形成雖由高點回落，但仍維持正成長，外部需求疲軟導致
出口季減，加上企業去庫存，顯示內需支撐正與貿易逆風強烈拉鋸。
三月份歐元區經濟活動連續 15 個月處於擴張區間，其中製造業表現強勁，歐元區製造業PMI 由
50.8 上升至 51.4，創 45 個月高點，受新訂單增長支撐，惟出口訂單連續第 49 個月低於榮枯線，外

需疲軟仍未改善。服務業受中東戰爭引發的不確定性與能源成本飆升衝擊，驟降至 50.1，放緩至 10
個月低點。預料南歐以觀光業為主國家將面對能源成本衝擊，而外銷為主的如德國、法國將迎來能源
與運輸成本飆升的雙重打擊。歐盟決定延後提交原定 4/15 提出將俄羅斯石油進口永久禁令入法的提
案，自 2022 年俄羅斯全面入侵烏克蘭以來，歐盟已大幅降低對俄能源依賴，是否因為伊朗戰爭調整
制裁內容將是未來關注重點。
歐洲央行在 3 月利率決策會議決定利率不變、但態度轉鷹。歐洲央行總裁拉加德表示，若通膨出
現「顯著但不過於持久」的超標情況，可能進行「適度的政策調整」，以確保通膨預期維持穩定。她
強調 ， 若對通膨偏離目標完全不作反應 ， 恐影響政策溝通與市場信心 ， 使外界難以理解央行反應機制 。
從利率期貨觀察 ， 市場預期歐洲央行與英國央行都有機會在第二季升息1 碼 ， 不排除未來一年升息到
2 碼，顯示歐洲央行與英國央行面對能源危機採取警戒態度。
在地緣風險常態化背景下，歐洲各國持續提高國防支出，德國政府規劃在2026 年投入 380 億歐

元於基礎建設、氣候保護專項基金、國防及數位化，加上 AI 浪潮帶動企業投資，投資者原本對歐洲
經濟抱持樂觀態度，然而油價大漲大幅扭轉市場樂觀預期。 考量市場目前悲觀原因仍是地緣政治風
險，假設美伊衝突能在第二季落幕，對歐股企業獲利影響有限，歐洲經濟將在下半年重回成長軌道。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.14，故價淨值比為 2.27。就評價角度相對合理，雖然年初以來歐
股的樂觀情緒因為伊朗戰爭快速扭轉，但未來若油價回落，或是歐洲重新考慮向俄羅斯購買能源，都
能夠化解歐洲面臨的能源困境，故仍可視為長線佈局標的。

日本股市：高市政府加大戰略產業投資，政府補貼壓抑油價衝擊
日本受惠於企業強勁支出 ，2025 年第四季 GDP 年化季增率為 1.3%， 優於原先預估的0.2%。2026
年不僅企業資本支出動能持續 ， 在高市早苗出任首相後 ， 政府選定支持包含實體人工智慧 、 再生醫學 、
量子運算與海洋無人機等 61 個項目，目標在 2040 年前取得全球 AI 機器人逾 30%的市占率，國產
晶片銷售額也要在同年衝上 40 兆日圓。高市希望藉由政府與民間的合作推動投資，強化發展包含人
工智慧、半導體、宇宙及航太等 17 大重點產業。由於自民黨已在國會選舉取得空前壓倒性勝利，產

業補貼計畫將可落實，讓政府投資不僅貢獻短期 GDP，亦可提升日本長期經濟競爭力。
日本 2 月剔除生鮮食品的消費者價格年比上漲 1.6%，創 2022 年 3 月以來最小升幅，主要因為
公用事業補貼壓低能源成本。日本勞動組合總連合會(連合)近期公布 2026 年春鬥第 1 次彙整結果，
加薪幅度雖低於去年同期 、 不過已連3 年高於 5%，且2026 年中小企業加薪幅度可望高於過去幾年，
支撐日本薪資物價形成正向循環，並達成實質薪資增長。近期伊朗戰事導致油價飆升，首相高市早苗
積極應對，除釋出戰備儲油外，將控制汽油價格上限，故預料短期通膨不致大幅躍昇。
日本央行三月利率決策會議維持政策不變，主要考量來自中東局勢不確定性，難以衡量伊朗戰爭
及國際油價飆漲對日本經濟及通膨帶來影響 ，呼應日本央行總裁植田和男論點 ， 希望有更多時間可以
觀察對物價的引導。後市央行升息時點須留意4 月初將公布的短觀報告，若企業信心尚維持正面，搭
配伊朗戰爭如白宮所預期的在 5、6 周之內結束，則日本央行或有望在第二季升息一碼。預料升息進

度放緩將使十年期日本公債殖利率在 2.0~2.5%區間震盪，且由於全球央行方向轉鷹、美元受惠於避
險需求，日本央行緩步升息有助日圓匯率穩定。
波克夏將對東京海上進行出資 ， 在4 月藉由收購東京海上持有的自家庫藏股 、 取得東京海上2.49%
股權，出資額為 2,874 億日圓。在巴菲特掌舵時期，波克夏對日本企業的投資以大型商社為主，此次
將是波克夏首度和日本金融機構進行合作 ， 性質與過去投資商社股不同 ， 若波克夏未來對日投資可擴
及其他非商社股，對於日股長期表現將帶來更大力度的加持。
2026 年以來日股受惠於高市擴大財政刺激 、強化軍事安全、鞏固美日同盟上，但本益比仍在 18.38
倍，股價淨值比在 1.68 倍。3 月下旬外資賣超日股，估計是受到伊朗戰事干擾且避免股利發放的課
稅而調整持股部位所致。近期日圓震盪走貶，有利於於日本外銷股財報表現，預料企業獲利可望為日
股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

新興亞洲股市：中東戰事對經濟影響負面，但成長仍具韌性
亞洲股市 3 月因中東情勢緊張而大跌，截至 3/27，亞洲不含日本指數 (MXAPJ) 單月下跌
10.39%。南韓股市跌幅居前，單月下跌 12.90%，多次於盤中啟動熔斷機制，除了中東地緣政治風
險之外，漲幅過大後的技術性修正也是主因。台股在內資支撐下，跌幅相對較輕，單月下跌 6.50%。
中國股市也表現弱勢，上證指數下跌 5.99%，國企指數下跌 4.58%。3 月份正值全國兩會結束，股市
出現了「利多出盡」的調節。MSCI 東協指數 3 月下跌 3.53%，是跌幅較輕的亞洲區域。馬來西亞與

印尼身為原油、液化天然氣（LNG）及棕櫚油的重要出口國，油價上漲直接帶動其相關權值股走強，
抵銷了跌幅，但越南、菲律賓等原油進口國，跌幅超過 10%。印度 SENSEX 指數下跌 9.48%，印度
是全球第三大石油進口國，約 85% 的原油依賴進口，油價高漲對經濟造衝擊，另外外資於三月大量
賣出，也是印度股市重挫的原因。
亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 將面對能源價格的劇烈波動對經濟的衝擊。根據 ADB 資料，泰國、
南韓與新加坡及印度等國，其能源進口依賴度顯著高於區域平均 。在當前中東衝突陰影未散的背景
下，油價的每一次跳動都觸發亞洲通膨的扳機，對於這些原油高依賴進口國家而言，能源成本的上升
不僅僅是物價問題，更是對 GDP 增長動能的直接侵蝕。
根據野村的推估「油價每上漲 10%」對各國經濟的衝擊路徑各不相同，印度與菲律賓的 CPI 通
膨壓力最為顯著，預計分別跳升 0.5 個百分點，這將迫使兩國央行在 Q2 維持較高利率環境，壓縮
降息空間 。反觀馬來西亞與印尼，受惠於其資源生產國的地位，GDP 增長甚至可能出現微幅的正向

回饋或較小的負面衝擊 。這種「能源非對稱性」將導致亞洲內部經濟與貨幣政策的進一步分化。
然而亞洲經濟在 2026 年 Q2 面臨增長放緩的挑戰。但根據博鰲亞洲論壇報告表示，受國際貿易不
確定性及地緣政治風險上升影響，今年亞洲增長率預計從去年的 4.7% 降至 4.5%。儘管增速微降，
但亞洲經濟體 GDP 占世界比重持續上升，依然是全球經濟的主要成長引擎。這與 ADB 資料相呼
應，顯示亞洲在全球經濟增速放緩的大環境下，憑藉區域一體化進程展現出較佳的韌性 。
對於印度與菲律賓等高通膨敏感區，應關注其內需韌性能否抵銷能源稅的負面影響；而對於新加
坡、南韓與台灣等雖然進口佔比較高但產業鏈地位穩固的經濟體，仍看好在全球 AI 供應鏈帶來的快
速增長。整體而言亞洲國家在 2026 年的經濟增長整體保持韌性，但將出現明確分化。

中國股市：轉型陣痛與外部地緣風險的夾擊
2026 年中國兩會不僅發佈經濟目標，更是「十五五規劃」首年的轉型宣言。官方將 GDP 成長
目標設定在 4.5% 至 5.0% 的區間，這項數據釋放了一個明確信號，北京已不再盲目追求過往的高
速增長，而是將重心置於「新質生產力」的質變與落地。在政策宣示中，我們觀察到由「總量擴張」
向 「結構優化」 的顯著移轉。2026 年數位經濟核心產業佔 GDP 比重目標提升至 12.5%，重點布局
人工智能（AI Agent）與具身智能（Physical AI）的工業化應用。同時，政府推動的大規模「設備更

新與消費品以舊換新」 政策，試圖在傳統房產動能消退之際，透過激發存量市場的替代需求來維持內
需熱度。這種以新經濟與高品質消費為主的政策組合，旨在對沖房地產下行對內需產生的結構性缺
口，並賦予國家經濟轉型的重任。
中國經濟正經歷一場歷史性的動能切換，長期作為增長引擎的房地產，其最終需求對 GDP 的佔
比正持續回落。與此同時，以醫藥、先進設備、IT 通訊及研發為首的「高科技 + 新三樣」產業，其
貢獻權重已經在 2025 年正式超越房地產。轉型過程雖然伴隨著陣痛，卻是中國邁向科技自主的必要
路徑 。 儘管房地產的長尾效應仍在消化 ， 但這些新興領域的快速擴張正為中國經濟建立更具韌性的基
礎，成為觀察中國能否維持區域增速預期的核心支柱。對於投資者而言，這意味著舊有的投資邏輯已
徹底改變，未來市場的超額收益將高度集中在具備全球競爭力的先進製造龍頭。
儘管新經濟轉型積極，但房地產市場的下滑壓力依然是中國最沉重的負擔。根據彭博模型，房地
產尋底之路極為漫長。預測路徑顯示，未來幾年房地產佔 GDP 比重將持續收縮至 12% 左右。中

國的政策博弈重點在於政府如何透過擴張性財政手段來進行 「止血」 ，防止房市超跌引發系統性金融
風險。但在目前市場信心修復極為緩慢的背景下，觀察銷售數據能否在超跌區間出現初步止穩。
然而中國面臨的外部環境更加嚴峻。中東危機的突然升溫，成為干擾復甦的關鍵變數。地緣震盪
推升了能源溢價，加劇輸入型通膨壓力，美國聯準會（Fed）態度的轉向鷹派與美元持續走強，顯著
壓縮了中國央行的降息空間。在強美元與高利差的雙重擠壓下，人民幣匯率面臨巨大的貶值壓力。這
種「內需待援、外部受限」的政策困局，使得操作空間顯得倍加受限，雖然兩會釋放了擴張性財政的
積極信號，但在地緣政治風險與強勢美元背景下，市場缺乏強勁的資金流入動能，也因此我們認為中
國股市欠缺全面性上漲的機會。類股建議在配置上規避傳統房地產產業，鎖定受惠於新質生產力政
策、具備技術護城河且能抵禦外部波動的科技領先標的。

印度股市： 中東戰事與 Fed 轉鷹影響印度成長動能
印度的實體經濟活動成長發出趨緩信號。3 月份最新發布的 HSBC Flash 印度綜合 PMI 指數由 2
月的 58.9 降至 56.5，創下 2022 年 10 月以來最弱的擴張速度，反映出國內需求在物價壓力下正
逐步降溫 。其中，製造業 PMI 降至 53.8，顯示國內訂單成長受阻；然而，值得關注的是國際訂單
卻創下調查歷史新高，這證明在全球供應鏈重組的戰略大環境下，印度製造的外部需求依然堅挺 。
儘管基本面仍具韌性，但 PMI 所揭示的投入成本增速創多年新高，預示著企業利潤空間將面臨嚴峻

的輸入型通膨擠壓，這將是觀察印度經濟能否維持高成長的首要指標。
儘管短期數據出現波動，但印度作為全球增長引擎的核心地位並未動搖。在 2026-27 財政預算
案的架構下， 政府持續推動以基礎設施投資為核心的擴張性政策 ， 試圖透過提升公共支出效率來強化
製造業基礎。印度正積極利用其人口紅利與政策激勵，吸引從電子組裝到半導體測試的全球業者進
駐。然而，這種高度依賴資本流入與外部能源供給的增長模式，在地緣政治動盪與聯準會轉向鷹派的
環境下，正迎來一場壓力測試。「內部政策利多」與「外部環境利空」的互相干擾，使得印度的成長
前景短期面臨不確定性 。
能源價格震盪是印度經濟短期最重要的潛在風險。根據彭博經濟的報導，印度央行估計，原油價
格每上漲 10 美元，經濟成長將會下降 0.15%。若中東衝突導致霍爾木茲海峽關閉三個月，2027 財
年成長率恐重挫至 6.1% 。在極端情況下，燃料成本飆升將引發連鎖反應，不僅削弱消費者的實際收
入，更會因國營石油公司虧損削減股利，進而擠壓預算案中的公共建設支出與政府投資能力。除了價

格波動，供應端的物理性中斷亦是威脅。印度的能源約 85%依賴進口，若航運中斷導致供應缺口，
包含民生消費與石化產業等經濟活動都將受到供應鍊斷裂的風險。
在貨幣政策上 ， 印度央行面臨 「內外夾擊」 。 若油價衝向140 美元 ，CPI 預計將進一步升 5.0%，
並可能在 9 月突破 RBI 的 6%容忍區間，央行被迫在年底臨時升息 2 碼。外部方面，聯準會態度的轉
向鷹派加劇了資金回流美元資產的壓力，也將干擾印度央行的寬鬆節奏。綜合上述分析，我們對印度
市場看法轉向中性。雖然結構性紅利與財政擴張確保了其增長基本盤 ， 但近期的重心已由 「追求擴張」
轉向「防禦通膨與能源衝擊」，這讓 PMI 顯示內需趨緩。加上潛在的油價的大漲與供應減少，正大
幅抵銷供應鏈轉移的長期利多。我們建議在股市配置上採取守勢。在近期的估值修正期，利用市場震
盪，重新評估基本面。

拉美股市：巴西正式啟動降息循環，中東衝突有利拉美經濟
巴西央行貨幣政策委員會(Copom)於 3 月 18 日會議全票通過將基準利率調降 25 個基點至
14.75%，正式啟動新一輪降息循環。Copom 會議紀要強調該決定與當前高度不確定性的背景相符，
在地緣政治衝突持續時間與範圍難以預判 、 經濟活動放緩步調及其對物價水準影響的訊號錯綜複雜之
下，保持從容並收集額外資訊為適當之舉。Copom 也為下次會議將降息步調加速至 50 個基點保
留空間，但也強調需美伊戰爭等外部環境改善且這些事件對通膨與通膨預期影響有限才能達成。

市場通膨預期方面，截至 3 月 30 日當周巴西央行市場調查顯示，2026 年通膨預期上升 14
個基點至 4.31%，高於巴西央行 3.0% 的長期通膨目標中點，2027、2028 及 2029 年則分別為
3.84%、3.57% 及 3.50%，惟有鑑於近期油價持續走升， 預計未來數週通膨預期將可能進一步惡化。
此外，市場對 2026 年底 Selic 利率中位預期維持在 12.50%，2027 及 2028 年底分別為 10.50%
及 10.00%，顯示雖中東戰爭帶動市場通膨升溫，然而巴西市場整體降息格局並未受到太大影響，仍
是朝大幅降息的方向前進。
另一方面，雖美伊戰爭帶動短線油價上漲，帶動拉美市場通膨預期，然而油價上漲事實上也有利
於拉美整體經濟，主因拉美市場中，包含阿根廷、巴西、哥倫比亞與厄瓜多等國為石油淨出口國，高
盛研究指出若油價上漲 10%，能使整體拉美市場未來 12 個月 GDP 上升約 0.2%，顯示美伊戰爭
對拉美市場來說並非全然只有負面影響。
最後一個值得關注的議題是巴西 2026 年的總統大選，市場主要關注現任總統盧拉，與前總統

波索納洛之子弗拉維奧的對決。市調機構 Quaest 3 月民調結果顯示，在第二輪決選中，兩位候選人
首度打成平手，各獲得 41% 支持度，扭轉過往皆由盧拉勝出的局勢，顯示中東衝突引發的能源價格
上漲與通膨壓力持續影響盧拉政權的支持度 。市場預期若最終總統大選由右派陣營的弗拉維奧勝出，
其政策將會有利於巴西財政赤字問題改善，相較於盧拉政營財政擴張的策略 ，更可能吸引外資流入巴
西市場。
展望後市 ，儘管中東衝突帶動拉美市場通膨預期攀升 ，然而拉美市場最大經濟體巴西仍處於降息
循環中，且目前市場並不預期巴西央行會因為能源價格問題，而扭轉寬鬆方向，此外，拉美市場整體
亦會受惠於油價上漲帶動的石油出口助益，故整體而言，我們目前對拉美市場後市仍抱持正向看法。

能源類股：美伊停戰協商短期難有結論，可以資產配置角度布局能源類股
美伊衝突仍是 3 月主導能源價格走勢的關鍵，雖然美國在 3 月 25 日透過巴基斯坦向伊朗提出停
戰協議，然而內容與伊朗訴求相差甚遠，預料短期內美伊達成停戰協議的可能性並不高，戰事仍可能
需要來回數次的武力攻擊與談判後，才能底定。
隨戰爭持續，荷莫茲海峽的中斷程度仍是油價最關鍵的變數，戰前荷莫茲海峽每天約有 130 艘
船通過海峽 ， 目前每天僅有不到 6 艘在伊朗許可下通行，海峽運輸仍幾乎處於癱瘓狀態 。 對伊朗而言 ，
封鎖荷莫茲海峽越久 ， 越能為其換得更好的談判籌碼 ， 伊朗國會目前更是在推動立法對通過海峽的船

舶收取過路費，以此將海峽視為經濟收入來源，故何時荷莫茲海峽能夠暢通，仍具高度不確定性。
受荷莫茲海峽運量持續受阻影響，高盛 3 月 23 日報告再度上修油價預測，預估 3 月布蘭特原油
平均價格將達 105 美元，4 月將進一步上升至 115 美元，並在 2026 年第四季回落至 80 美元，該預
測基於荷莫茲海峽石油流量將在 6 週內維持極低水準的假設。此外，在海峽流量中斷 10 週的不利情
境下，高盛預估布蘭特油價將可能接近或超越 2008 年 147 美元的歷史紀錄，隨後在2026 年第四季
放緩至 100 美元；在中斷10 週並同時伴隨基礎設施受損的嚴重不利情境下，油價可能飆至接近160
美元，並在2026 年第四季仍維持 115 美元高油價。在高盛各種情境分析中，若海峽流量中斷時間延
長，或者有更多能源基礎設施受損，長期油價都存在上升的可能性，有利能源類股表現。
另一方面，觀察 Bloomberg 西德州原油期貨遠期曲線，可以看出目前市場仍定價荷莫茲海峽受
阻為中短期事件，曲線在開戰後前端快速上揚，反映現貨端供給緊張狀況，然而遠端合約變動幅度不

大，且近幾個月合約曲線陡峭，顯示市場認為荷莫茲海峽受阻仍將是短期事件，並不預期戰爭將會演
變成長期戰事，預料隨衝突結束，原油供給將大幅恢復，帶動油價下跌。3 月 30 日 WTI 遠期曲線顯
示，油價將會在 11 月回落至每桶 80 美元以下。此外，截至 3/30，國際賭盤 Polymarket 預估美伊
在 4/15 前停戰機率約 17%，4/30 前為 32%，6/30 前為 59%，12/31 前則為 75%， 機率在 4/30
至 6/30 區間增幅最大，隱含市場對停火的預期主要落在 4~6 月。
展望後市，雖然近期美伊皆提出談判條件，然而雙方在談判條件上仍存在顯著落差，再加上對於
伊朗來說，將荷莫茲海峽封鎖的越久，讓能源價格維持高檔，越能為伊朗得到更好的談判籌碼，故預
估伊朗更可能採取邊打邊談的策略，將整體談判時程拉長。對於能源類股來說，短線上若戰爭持續，
能源類股有望走出獨立行情，並可作為投資組合中對衝戰爭風險的角色，而即使在停戰後，長期油價
仍有望高於戰前水準，亦有利支撐能源類股表現，故建議可以資產配置角度，適度布局能源類股。

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：美伊戰事壓抑短線金價表現，惟反轉條件已逐步成形
黃金傳統上被視為戰爭期間的避險資產，然而本波自 2 月 28 日美伊戰爭爆發以來，金價卻明顯
表現不佳，從戰前約 5,100 美元一路下跌，一度回撤至約 4,100 美元位置。本波金價走疲，主因美伊
戰爭導致荷莫茲海峽受阻，持續推升油價，帶動市場通膨預期上升，同步推高了美國公債殖利率，使
不孳息的黃金資產吸引力下降。此外，市場避險情緒亦帶動美元走強，而黃金價格長期以來與美元走
勢呈負相關，隨美元指數走升，黃金等以美元計價之商品價格亦出現承壓。
此外，黃金重要買盤全球央行，近期購金動能也出現明顯放緩。世界黃金協會報告指出，2026

年 1 月全球央行淨購金量驟降至 5 噸，較去年月均購金量 27 噸銳減近 80%，推測主因 1 月金價波
動過大，導致各國央行態度轉為觀望。 另一方面，土耳其央行近期大幅減持黃金 ，也對市場情緒造
成壓抑。據彭博新聞引述知情人士報導，土耳其央行在 3 月 13 日至 3 月 20 日的兩周內，以出售及
協議交換置換了約 60 噸黃金，用以穩定因美伊戰爭而受影響的土耳其里拉匯率，彭博指出，土耳其
央行本次減持黃金，一部分是採市場直接拋售，一部分則是通過 SWAP 以黃金作為抵押品來獲取外
匯或里拉 ，而歷史上各國央行透過 SWAP 以貴金屬獲得廉價美元融資的情況並不少見，且回顧 2023
年 3 月至 5 月期間，土耳其央行亦曾售出近 160 噸黃金，惟當時因來自黃金 ETF 的買家吸收賣壓，
對短線金價並未造成太大影響，故若僅有來自土耳其央行的一次性賣壓，實際並不需要太過擔憂。
更需要留意的是 ， 中東戰爭將可能迫使其他國家同樣採取出售黃金儲備的動作，以支撐外匯存底
或籌措資金，若此趨勢擴大，將 可能進一步增加黃金短線賣壓， 同時，因目前市場資金較不偏好黃

金，短線上黃金 ETF 吸納來自各國央行賣壓的力道也會較弱，故只要美伊戰爭持續，黃金類股短線
表現將可能受到較大壓力。
即便如此，我們仍可看出世界央行對中長期增持黃金儲備抱持正向態度 ，例如在 1 月份的世界央
行黃金買家中，就意外的出現了馬來西亞央行，為該國自 2018 年以來首度增持黃金儲備，另外在 3
月初，韓國央行也宣布計畫從 2026 年第一季起，將黃金 ETF 納入其外匯準備投資組合，顯示各國
央行對於增持黃金儲備的長期方向仍未改變 。世界黃金協會也預估 ，預期全球央行在 2026 年將會增
持約 850 噸黃金，增持水準將會與去年相近，顯示未來世界央行購金買盤仍可望為金價帶來支撐。
此外，展望後市 ，金價反轉的條件實際上也已在逐步成形，當前市場直覺認為高油價將導致通膨 ，
預期聯準會動作會偏鷹，導致金價表現受到壓抑。然而若戰事在短期內結束，預料市場將忽略短期
油價上漲的影響，使降息預期大致回升到戰前水準，有利金價表現；而若美伊戰爭 進一步擴大，將
可能使油價被推升至壓抑經濟表現的價格，屆時經濟更可能面臨的問題反而不是通膨而是衰退， 聯

準會更可能降息以救經濟，隨利率走低亦有利金價反彈。故 目前來看，隨金價回檔已深，重回多頭
條件也已在逐步成形，建議短線可持續觀望戰事變化，中性看待黃金類股後市表現。

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：通膨疑慮雖推升利率，未來仍有降息可能
FOMC 3/18 會議釋出訊號略偏鷹派。投票結果 11 比 1，僅 Miran 主張降息一碼，而原先偏鴿
的 Waller 轉為支持按兵不動，內部對降息的共識正在弱化。會後聲明雖新增中東局勢不確定性的描
述，釋出政策需保持耐心的訊號，但整體對經濟評估仍維持「穩健」 ，未因2 月就業數據轉弱而顯著
調整措辭。點陣圖中位數雖未變動，但分布結構出現轉變。首先，預期降息的官員人數減少，支持多
次降息的比例下滑；其次，已出現個別官員預期未來升息的情境；此外，長期利率中位數上修，顯示

中性利率評估提高。
雖然因中東能源問題導致 Fed Funds 隔夜利率交換市場所估算今年底前的利率變動預期已從戰
事爆發前的降息 2 碼大幅縮減，近期更出現不排除升息的臆測。然而，自美以伊衝突以來至 3/26(台
北時間)， 包括高盛在內的投資銀行所發布對Fed 利率(區間上緣)預期均認為 FOMC 未來兩年仍將傾
向降息。若此預期得以實現，代表市場目前已過度反映 Fed 的「鷹派情境」，之後殖利率可望有回
穩的空間。
再者，3 月油價上揚，投資人對於未來美國貨幣政策轉向的疑慮升溫，確實也導致了美國甚至是
全球的主要債券市場殖利率上揚。然而，就在市場聚焦於通膨的同時，包括 Pimco、摩根大通和
Columbia Threadneedle Investments 等投資機構 3/30 不約而同表達，投資人低估了美國對伊朗
戰爭導致經濟急劇放緩的風險的看法，並認為這場衝擊最終將引發債券殖利率再度回落 。高盛 3 月下
旬的報告提醒，比起升息，中東戰事更大的風險在於(接近)經濟衰退，這可能觸發 Fed 更大幅度的降

息。Pimco 首席投資官 Daniel Ivascyn 也表示「通常始於通膨衝擊的局面，會迅速演變為成長衝擊…
而我們正處於經濟大幅走軟的邊緣。」因此，未來債券殖利率不排除有向下的可能性存在。
隨著美國 11 月期中選舉逼近，川普在政治上將面臨更大的壓力，以美國財政體質及川普向來的
政策來看，之後美國政府勢必將盡力壓低美國公債的殖利率，有利使美債利率處於相對可控的範圍。
近期美國公債殖利率雖因市場的通膨疑慮而上揚 ， 但目前殖利率遠高於Fed 長期通膨目標(2%)，
美債的高息收對長線投資人具強大誘因 ， 因此投資人仍可以資產配置角度對於債券作適當之配置 。 此
外，包括 Pimco、摩根大通等投資機構也認為未來債券殖利率有向下的可能性存在。以目前的殖利
率看，美債仍有投資空間。考量短期殖利率受市場對 Fed 的利率政策預期變動影響較大，故在年期
與券種的選擇上可以 7~15 年的中長期美國公債或投資級債為主。

解读
一、7 月美国 CPI 环比转为持平，能源价格下降是主要原因。
7 月美国 CPI 环比持平，为 0，比 6 月提高 0.3 个百分点，季
调后，环比上涨 0.1%，比 6月提高 0.5个百分点。

源价格环比下降 1.2%，降幅比 6 月收窄 3.7 个百分点；核心 CPI
环比上涨 0.1%，比 6月提高 0.1个百分点。
油价环比下跌。7 月国际油价（布伦特原油价格）均价 80.22
美元/桶，比 6 月均价（84.85 美元/桶美元）下降 5.46%；WTI 原
油价格平均为 78.23 美元/桶，比 6 月均价（80.64 美元/桶美元）
下降 2.99%。

Seventy years ago today, the curtain came down on the summer workshop
that gave birth to artificial intelligence. Dwight Eisenhower was in the White
House, the Suez Crisis was gripping Britain and Elvis Presley topped the US
charts with “Don’t Be Cruel” and “Hound Dog”.
The Dartmouth Summer Research project brought together a dozen or so

academics convinced that every aspect of intelligence could in principle be
described precisely enough for a machine to simulate it. Emboldened by the
techno-optimism of the age, John McCarthy, who coined the name AI, wrote
that “we think that a significant advance can be made… if a carefully
selected group of scientists work on it together for a summer.”
Since then, AI has lived a lifetime of summers and winters, punctuated by
stunning breakthroughs and false dawns. As it is now the cornerstone of a

new investment and valuation boom, the past 70 years are a treasure trove
of clues for what to expect in the future.
To paraphrase the research paper that sparked the current boom in
generative AI in 2017, “Attention is all you need”, you could say “context is
all you need” to understand what is to come. The signals are there, from
non-linear growth to infrastructure constraints and valuation booms and
busts.
Some will say “this time is different”. Maybe. But the following charts give a

flavour of what could happen if it isn’t. To quote the UK No. 1 single from 70
years ago, sung by Doris Day: “Whatever Will Be, Will Be (Que Sera, Sera)”.
Artificial intelligence marks 70 years since Dartmouth Summer Research Project
“Context is all you need”: clues from the past for what comes next
1. AI progress is non-linear
2. Progress exceeds Moore's law
3. Today's leading tech may not be
tomorrow's
4. The pipeline matters
5. Cheaper AI will likely boost

spending
6. Software revolutions need
hardware
7. Hardware is often a bottleneck
8. Globalisation has made tech
cheaper, and more at risk
9. Tech revolutions take time to pay
10. Consumers have adopted AI faster
than businesses
11. The biggest valuation booms are
often tech booms
12. This time is different – and will
need to be
13. Early leads do not guarantee long-
term winners
14. Long-term trends tend to persist
14 lessons from a lifetime of boom and bust

2 Deutsche Bank Research, Adrian Cox, Thematic Strategist, August 2026

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Bond yields have been rising in recent weeks, especially
at the long end of the maturity spectrum. We have long
believed that the global structure of interest rates would
have a higher centre of gravity in future than it did in
the period between the GFC and the pandemic. The

factors often cited for the recent rise in yields – larger
government deficits globally, the investment boom from
AI – were already behind this judgement.
• The question then arises: have we allowed enough for
these forces? Getting the direction right is one thing;
quantifying the impact is another. In doing so, though,
we must take care not to confuse trend and cycle. Some
of the recent rise stems from the exceptional demand
for funding data centre construction. This effect of the

technology wave could last a while, but it is more a
cyclical development than a permanent structural one.
• For the Australian economy, developments at the long end
of the yield curve tend to be less relevant. Most financing
happens at the short end of the maturity spectrum, especially
for households. But there are borrowers and investors who
are affected by long-dated yields, especially governments.
• Higher yields mean higher debt-servicing costs,

particularly for the US government. Expect more
manoeuvres like the US Treasury supporting the yen
by selling euros not dollars, as well as the buy-back
operation this week. These are merely stop-gaps rather
than a lasting solution like genuine fiscal consolidation.
Westpac Economics has long held the house view that the
global structure of interest rates would be higher in the future
than it was in the period between the GFC and the pandemic.
Central bank policy might influence the short end, but over a

longer period, what matters for interest rates globally is the
global balance of saving and investment. Pre-pandemic, saving
rates were high, thanks in part to official sectors accumulating
reserves. Meanwhile, investment was weak. Confidence had
been frayed by the crisis, and some governments were in
austerity mode. That decade’s new technologies did not spark
the same kind of productivity-enhancing investment boom
as computers and the internet did in the late 1990s and early

2000s. And in any case, banks in North America and Europe
were recapitalising and less inclined to lend.
There were good reasons to see that period as an aberration,
though, including the very long-term evidence we noted two
years ago. As we highlighted at the time, a range of forces
have been pushing “neutral” rates higher, along with the
Higher bond yields: not just one thing
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
structure of interest rates more broadly. These include higher

government spending as populations age, as well as the
private sector needing to invest in both energy transition and
AI. Higher defence spending was also in the mix, especially
after Germany changed its constitution last year to allow
more deficit spending for this purpose. The general fiscal
incontinence of the US government was already evident two
years ago and worsened under the current administration. All
these factors imply greater bond supply, and so higher yields.

To unpack this further, a bond yield is normally thought of as
being the combination of several components that are easier
to name than to measure. Nominal government bond yields
are composed of (expected) inflation plus real yields, where
real yields are normally assumed to reflect expected future
(real) policy rates plus a “term premium”. The term premium
is in principle the compensation investors require for the risk
that things turn out differently from their expectations of the

other two components, and in practice is a residual grab bag
of everything else that might affect demand and supply in
the bond market. Corporate bonds add a risk spread to this,
reflecting that a company might default but a sovereign issuing
in its own currency will never need to.
The higher global structure of interest rates can be seen in all
these components. Inflation generally undershot central bank
targets in the 2010s; since the pandemic, it has at best been

around target, with global supply shocks periodically lifting it
above target. Real yields are higher, and with shorter-maturity
bonds now paying positive real yields, we are seeing less of
the “search for yield” behaviour that compressed both term
premia and risk spreads in the 2010s.
But how much?
It is one thing to say rates will be higher on average. We also
need to ask: by how much? This is where the AI boom comes
in. The scale of the planned global investment in data centres

is nothing short of mind-boggling, and it is creating substantial
demand for debt finance. The “hyperscaler” tech firms
(Alphabet, Amazon, Meta and Microsoft) used to be enormous
cash generators. Now they are responsible for some of the
largest debt issuances in the market, including Alphabet’s
“rumblings of investor discontent
about the US fiscal position have
become evident in yields."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
super-sized $A deal this week. Issuance in Australia and other
non-US jurisdictions makes sense for these firms. They want to
access a diversified pool of investors, and many of the assets
they are financing are outside the US, especially now that data

centres have become unpopular in many US communities.
The scale of this greater investment call on global savings is one
reason to expect longer-dated yields to average even higher in
the near term than their range since the pandemic so far.
We must take care, though, to distinguish trend from cycle.
This is particularly relevant for the data centre boom, which is
likely to be a big thing for the next few years, but settle down
as the technology matures and computing capacity expands

(and becomes cheaper).
The US government is also making an exceptional call on global
saving, and this looks more like a longer-term structural trend than
the AI boom does. The US federal government has been running
deficits in excess of 4% of GDP for a decade, aside from a brief
period in 2022, and more than 6% since the beginning of 2023.
In recent weeks, rumblings of investor discontent about the
US fiscal position have become evident in yields. And those

hyperscaler bonds, including the $A-denominated ones, are
offering a higher yield for companies with similar ratings to
the US sovereign. The bonds of governments in better fiscal
positions, including Australia’s, also start looking attractive.
To this we must add that Japan now looks to be becoming a
“normal” economy with positive inflation and interest rates,
and an undervalued currency that might tempt some investors
to bet on an appreciation. While it remains the case that There

Is No Alternative to the US Treasury market for depth and
liquidity, some diversification out of USD assets is starting to
look both more feasible and more attractive.
The other consequences
For the Australian economy, developments at the long end of the
yield curve tend to be less relevant than those at the short end.
Most financing happens at the short end of the maturity spectrum,
especially for households. But some borrowers and investors are

affected by long-dated yields, especially governments, and the
local firms involved in data centre construction.
Offshore, we can see higher yields constraining policy
decisions by the US government and can expect more on
this front. The US government’s debt-servicing costs are
becoming more burdensome as large deficits persist and
yields rise. Just in the past few weeks, we have seen some
unusual currency interventions by the US Treasury, followed

by buy-backs designed to shorten the maturity profile of US
government debt and lower the term premium paid. And while
we expect the Fed will not fold in the face of pressure to keep
rates low, we can well imagine the content of the phone calls
between FOMC Chair Warsh and President Trump. Expect
more manoeuvres by the US authorities along the lines seen
recently. These are merely stop-gaps, though, not lasting
solutions like genuine fiscal consolidation.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the foundations of confidence
Since the meeting, data has been constructive for the
disinflationary trend, and the majority of FOMC members
who have spoken have recognised the importance of the
Committee’s credibility on inflation but also that they have

time on their side in assessing conditions.
Turning to Asia, the July China data round underwhelmed yet
again. The return from trade is incredible, but retail sales growth
has essentially stalled, 0.6%yr, and the decline in fixed asset
investment is increasingly broad based, -6.7%ytd. House price
declines continue to weigh on household wealth, and domestic
equity holdings do not have the scale or breadth to compensate.
Authorities may be keeping quiet on stimulus ahead of the

next meeting between President Xi and President Trump, and
as the US looks to expand its economic actions against Iran
– potentially via third parties with ties to Iran, such as China.
But, very clearly, there is an urgent need for stimulus if the
2026 and 2027 growth targets are to be achieved.
After an upside surprise in June, the labour force survey
disappointed in July, reporting a loss of 15.8k jobs. This
leaves average monthly employment growth year-to-date

at 20.8k, above 2025’s 12.6k but below the pace needed
to keep the unemployment rate unchanged. It is therefore
unsurprising that the unemployment rate continued to edge
higher, reaching 4.5% in July, 0.4ppts above its level at the
turn of the year. Hours worked also disappointed, declining
0.6% in the month to be up just 0.2% over the year. Slowly
but surely, slack is forming in the labour market, weighing on
activity growth but supporting the disinflationary trend. That

trend must be sustained if the next move in the cash rate is to
be down as Westpac forecasts, albeit not until next August.
The Q2 wage price index was also favourable for the inflation
outlook. As expected, the headline wage price index rose
0.8% in Q2 to be 3.2% higher over the year. Private sector
wage growth is now at its weakest since Q4 2021 (0.7%qtr,
3.1%yr). Public sector wage momentum provided an offset
in Q2, gaining 0.9%qtr and 3.4%yr. The detail suggests that

a larger share of public sector jobs received a wage increase
than a year ago (25% versus 20%), but that the average hourly
wage increase for those jobs was smaller (3.1% versus 3.5%).
Australian consumers are becoming more aware of labour
market risks, with the Westpac-MI consumer sentiment  survey
unemployment index rising above its long-run average in
August. But the cumulative impact of cost-of-living pressures
remains the major impediment to confidence: both “family

finances versus a year ago” and “family finances next 12
months” stayed below average in August, despite mortgage
holders receiving a welcome reprieve from the RBA. And
improved comfort around family finances is only the first
step towards a recovery in consumer demand. “Time to buy a
major household item” remains 24% below average despite
a monthly bounce, while “time to buy a dwelling” is similarly
20% below average. We expect a lengthy period of weakness

in Australia’s economy, with a return to trend activity growth
not foreseen until late-2028.
Offshore, attention centred on FOMC messaging. Overall, the
July minutes showed a high degree of caution over the inflation
outlook, reflecting uncertainty around the Middle East conflict
and US economic policy, as well as an expectation that the
US economy would maintain its recent momentum. “Most
participants anticipated that inflation would step down over the

rest of the year as the effects of tariffs and earlier energy price
increases wane, but many participants noted the possibility that
inflation might be more persistently elevated.” In that context,
“many participants assessed that policy tightening would likely
be necessary if inflation did not decline”.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Counting down the days
This week saw a handful of data releases, with no decisive
signals for the RBNZ as it approaches its 2 September
Monetary Policy Statement. Market pricing is firmly in
favour of another OCR hike next month, but there is much

more uncertainty around what the RBNZ will signal for the
remaining meetings this year. We’ll be discussing these
issues and more in our latest quarterly Economic Overview,
released next Wednesday.
The monthly selected prices release, which covers almost half
of the quarterly CPI basket, was softer than expected in July.
Of particular note was a muted 0.1% rise in food prices for the
month, with annual growth slowing to an 18-month low of 1.9%.
Meat prices saw their largest monthly fall since 2020, and there

were also prices declines for wine and some dairy products.
Outside of food, petrol prices were down 6% over the month,
with diesel down 12%. However, the renewed hostilities in
the Middle East since July mean that prices at the pump have
been higher again over August. There were large increases in
domestic and international airfares, both of which experience
large seasonal swings, and are likely to ease back next month.
However, with global oil prices still elevated, this is one area

where we could see ongoing strength.
July also saw another 0.1% fall in housing rents, continuing
the weak trend seen over the past year. With weak population
growth and ample supply, housing rents are likely to remain
subdued for some time. Rents make up just over 10% of the
CPI, so they are having a significant dampening effect on
overall inflation at the moment.
Altogether, the July results signal some downside risk to
our existing forecast of a 0.7% rise in consumer prices in the

September quarter (which would see annual inflation slow down
from 4.1% to 3.7%). However, we’re still left with a picture of
inflation that’s likely to be well above the RBNZ’s target range for
the remainder of this year, including firm levels of core inflation.
The June quarter producer price index showed a range of cost
pressures, with input prices rising 2.9% and output prices up
1.6%. Higher fuel prices were an obvious driver, showing through

in a range of industries such as wholesaling, transport and
construction. As the June quarter CPI has already shown, though,
many firms have limited ability to pass on these cost increases.
Turning to the activity data, card spending at retail stores rose
by 1.3% in July, following and equal-sized fall in June. Indeed,
these figures have been volatile for several months even after
seasonal adjustment – there may have been some changes in
the timing of spending that are difficult to account for. Looking

through this, spending is up modestly on a year ago, but price
increases – especially for fuel – will have accounted for a large
part of this. We expect next week’s retail trade survey to show
a 0.2% fall in sales volumes for the June quarter.
The services PSI was little changed at 50.6 in July, holding just
above the zero-growth mark. So far this year the services survey
has been more subdued compared to the manufacturing survey,
which recorded a solid 54.3 in July after spiking to 60.1 in June.

That may reflect their relative exposures to the global market,
with strong demand and a favourable exchange rate boosting
export-oriented sectors and regions.
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
The second GlobalDairyTrade auction for August saw a 2.3%
rise in dairy prices, led by strong gains in whole milk and
skim milk powders. These results had been well signalled
by the futures market, which has seen a strong run-up in
prices in recent weeks. Longer-dated contracts – covering

the New Zealand summer – have risen particularly strongly,
suggesting that the market is eyeing the risk that a severe El
Niño event could restrict milk production this season. With
New Zealand accounting for a large share of internationally-
traded dairy products, past droughts have tended to squeeze
global prices higher, sometimes resulting in a net boost to
industry-wide earnings.
The final release this week was the goods trade data for July,
which fell further into deficit in seasonally adjusted terms.

Exports remained strong, supported by higher commodity
prices. But the country’s import bill has surged in the last few
months, mostly reflecting the jump in fuel prices. The July
figures also included a particularly large diesel shipment, for
the purpose of lifting our onshore fuel reserves in response to
the Middle East conflict. Outside of fuel, the ongoing growth
in imports of capital equipment suggests that firms have
remained willing to invest through the war uncertainty.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Jul Westpac-MI Leading Index 
Aug 26, Last: –0.36%
The Leading Index growth rate dropped to –0.36% in June from 
–0.25% in May. The update pointed to a more material loss of 
momentum although the growth pulse was still not overly weak. 
The July read include will again include a mixed bag of monthly 
updates with equity markets and commodity prices firming, 
dwelling approvals posting a solid rise and the yield spread 
and consumer expectations a little more positive but some 
softer signals emanating from labour market components: 
consumer unemployment expectations and total hours 
worked. All up the mix may see a slight improvement in the 
growth rate signal.

AUS: Jul Monthly CPI (%yr)
Aug 26, Last: 3.8, Westpac f/c: 3.3 
Mkt f/c: 3.3, Range: 3.1 to 3.6 
The June Monthly CPI was much better than feared, falling 
for a second consecutive month at –0.1%mth/3.8%yr. The 
monthly trimmed mean also moderated to 0.3%mth with a 
slight up tick in the year-ended pace of 3.6%yr.
We expect the July monthly CPI to rise 0.8%mth with the 
annual pace easing back to 3.3%yr. Holiday travel and auto 
fuel are the key drivers of the stronger monthly outcome, 
alongside a pick-up in some domestic market services resulting 
from the higher minimum wage decision. Electricity will be the 
main drag in the month. The monthly trimmed mean is forecast 
to rise 0.4%mth, taking the annual pace to 3.5%yr. For the full 
preview, see here.
AUS: Q2 Construction Work Done (%qtr)
Aug 26, Last: 3.4, Westpac f/c: –1.0 
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: –2.0 to 2.5
Construction activity grew 3.4%qtr in Q1, leaving it 6.3% 
higher over the year. However, the headline result was 
boosted by the installation of a floating production unit for 
the Scarborough Energy Project, much of which would have 
already been captured in the National Accounts through earlier 
progress payments. As this temporary spike unwinds, we 
expect construction activity to fall by around 1.0%qtr in Q2.
Beyond this, underlying trends remain more favourable. 
Public infrastructure investment is soft as major projects are 
completed, but residential construction is expected to recover 
following the unexpected Q1 decline. At the same time, data 
centre related developments and population-driven demand 
for commercial floorspace should continue to support non-
residential building activity.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Jul Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Aug 27, Last: 0.8, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Mkt f/c: 0.3, Range: –0.5 to 0.7
The household spending indicator rose 0.8%mth in June,
beating expectations. The gain followed a choppy run,
spending up 1.2%mth in May having fallen 1%mth in April.

Annual growth strengthened to 6.0%yr.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker  maintained solid
momentum through July and early August. However, the gains
were stronger in parts that are not covered by the ABS HSI
(including international transactions). A narrower measure that
more closely aligned with the HSI points to a dip in the July
month. Vehicle sales (covered by HSI but not the card tracker)
also look to have dipped after a strong June quarter. Overall

we expect the indicator to show a 0.5% decline with a small
downward revision to June also possible.
NZ: Q2 Retail Trade Survey (%qtr)
Aug 24, Volumes last: 0.9, Westpac f/c: –0.2
In the early part of this year, we were seeing signs that
spending appetites were lifting, with retail sales posting
several quarters of solid growth. However, that momentum
has taken a knock recently due to the large increase in fuel
prices and related uncertainty about the economy outlook.

We’re forecasting a 1.1% rise in nominal spending over the
June quarter. However, with a large rise in prices, volumes are
expected to be down 0.2%. Core spending is expected likely to
be firmer, with a moderate 0.6% rise forecast.
AUS: Q2 Private CAPEX (%qtr)
Aug 27, Last: 6.5, Westpac f/c: 0.0
Market f/c: 0.0, Range: –6.0 to 4.0
Private capex grew a very strong 6.5%qtr in Q1 2026, with
investment in machinery and equipment driving much of the
increase, primarily led by the IT sector. Capital imports suggest

there will be some payback in the machinery and equipment
component this quarter following that surge. However, there
has also been a significant increase in the construction of
commercial buildings with building approvals rising strongly
over the June quarter. Overall, this should see private capex
hold flat in Q2, while remaining elevated in level terms.
Est 6 for 2025/26 capex plans, after our adjustment, is
consistent with robust nominal growth of 11.2%yr and real

growth of 10.7%yr. We expect Est 3 unadjusted plans for
2026/27 to come in at $195bn, an 11.5% increase on a year
ago, pointing to continued growth in FY27.

NZ: Jul Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Aug 28, Last: 0.1, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has continued to tick higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result
for July – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% rise on the initial release,
although this measure tends to get revised down a bit in later
releases.

Document 222: Consumer Checkpoint_ The great convergence After more than a year of a K-shaped economy, consumers are looking increasingly alike as wage and spending gains converge. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Checkpoint_ The great convergence After more than a year of a K-shaped economy, consumers are looking increasingly alike as wage and spending gains converge. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-08-01
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

13006719     1

Economy 
Consumer Checkpoint: The great convergence 
11 August 2026

Key takeaways 
• Total card spending growth eased to 5.0% year-over-year (YoY) in July, from 6.3% in June, according to Bank of America internal 
data. But much of the moderation appears tied to the fading of temporary boosts (i.e., the timing of major online promotions 
and World Cup-related spending) rather than a broad deterioration in underlying demand. Spending ex-gas still rose a solid 4.3% 
YoY. 
• What was once a "K"-shaped consumer is increasingly becoming one of convergence, with spending growth converging across 
income groups. The exception remains the top 5% of earners, where strong balance sheets and rising asset prices continue to 
support outsized spending growth. 
• Against this backdrop, consumer financial health looks solid. Despite cost-of-living pressures from areas such as gasoline, the 
share of households paying off their credit card bills in full each month has risen. And there is little sign of an acceleration in 
households drawing upon their savings.

Spending growth eased in July as temporary boosts fell away 
Consumer spending growth eased a little in July, but the overall picture remains robust. Bank of America total aggregated credit 
and debit card spending per household increased 5.0% year-over-year (YoY), cooling from 6.3% YoY in June but still a top three 
reading in the past three years and over four times the 2025 average (Exhibit 1). Excluding gasoline, total spending rose 4.3% 
YoY in July, down from 5.6% YoY in June, but still above May levels. And while seasonally-adjusted spending per household fell 
0.2% month-over-month (MoM), this only partially erased the 0.6% increase observed in June.  
Exhibit 1: Spending growth eased in July to levels seen earlier this year – both with and without gasoline factored in 
Total card spending growth per household, based on Bank of America aggregated credit and debit card data (monthly, MoM%, seasonally adjusted (SA)) and 
(monthly, YoY%, non-SA, right-hand side (rhs))

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
July’s moderation in consumer spending growth was not surprising, as June spending was boosted by temporary factors. First, 
there was a timing mismatch in online promotions between 2026 and 2025, with online purchases pulled forward into June this 
year, whereas most occurred in July last year. Second, June was likely the peak of World Cup-related spending, so some cooling in

categories like restaurants and bars was to be expected. Third, fluctuations in global oil prices, and consequently gasoline prices,

However, even when we strip out gasoline and online retail purchases, Bank of America daily card data shows fairly consistent 
spending growth across the past two months (Exhibit 2). And although growth eased slightly in the middle of July, it recovered 
toward the end of the month.

The Great Convergence 
Over the past year, we have discussed at length the “K-shaped” divide between higher- and lower-income households’ spending 
and wage growth. But since May, our data has shown a significant narrowing in this gap. In fact, as of July, spending and wage 
growth have largely converged across income cohorts, with the exception of the top 5% of earners, who continue to outpace the 
rest.

Looking across income groups, lower-income households’ spending growth rose 5.4% YoY in July, while middle-income 
households slightly lagged, with an increase of 4.9% YoY (Exhibit 5). A similar dynamic was evident in discretionary spending 
(Exhibit 6).

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

In our view, one factor behind the narrowing spending growth gap is stronger after-tax wage growth. For lower- and middle-
income households, after-tax wage growth rose to 5.2% YoY and 4.2% YoY, respectively, in July (read more in The Institute 
Employment Report: July 2026) (Exhibit 7). These gains in wage growth have left lower- and middle-income households looking 
more “balanced,” with a narrower gap between spending and wage growth. In fact, that gap has effectively disappeared for 
lower-income households (Exhibit 8).   
Exhibit 7: In July, after-tax wage growth for lower-income 
households surpassed that of higher-income for the first time since 
December 2024

The equity market is likely still supporting higher-income households’ spending growth for now 
Looking specifically at the top 5% of households by income, we see something starkly different from the convergence seen 
elsewhere: a widening gap between their still-strong spending growth and easing wage growth (Exhibit 8). 
In our view, the decline in after-tax wage growth in this top 5% cohort is likely to be largely due to the fading impact of strong 
bonus payments earlier in the year. At the same time, their continued strong card spending is likely being driven by the wealth

effect from increases in investments made in more liquid forms of assets, such as directly held equities.

Airlines, lodging and clothing see the largest convergences

cohorts in general merchandise, lodging and durables (excluding electronics), the gap is most prominent in airlines and clothing 
(Exhibit 11).

Still, even while these large spending gaps persist in clothing and airlines, lower-income households have made significant 
progress in narrowing them over the past six months (Exhibit 12). And the gap has also narrowed meaningfully in lodging, 
durables and general merchandise stores.  
Conversely, lower earners now outpace higher earners in restaurant spending growth. Also, some of the narrowing – especially in 
clothing and lodging – is due to easing spending growth from higher-income households over the past two months.

Consumers generally remain in good credit and deposit health  
The current closing of the “K” is, in our view, likely a positive for overall consumer spending. This is particularly the case as the 
convergence in spending growth appears to be largely upwards, with lower- and middle-income households narrowing the gap 
towards higher-income peers.  
More broadly, the overall financial position of households continues to look strong. For example, Bank of America credit card 
data suggests that a higher share of households are paying off their full credit card balances each month across all income 
cohorts (Exhibit 13). While the share making only minimum payments has also increased, the rise is relatively small (Exhibit 14).

I NSTI TUTE

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts.  
Durables spending is defined as spending on electronics, building materials, auto-related spending and furniture. Premium durables spending is 
based on a selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively higher value products. Conversely, value durables spending is based on a 
selection of retailers who are judged to sell relatively lower value products.  
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

QUYẾT ĐỊNH 40/2026/QĐ-TTg  
Mở đường cho lộ trình thoái vốn giai đoạn 2026-2030 
 Nội dung của Quyết định 40 
— Ngày 05/08/2026, Thủ tướng Chính phủ đã ban hành Quyết định 40/2026/QĐ-TTg 
về tiêu chí phân loại doanh nghiệp nhằm thực hiện cơ cấu lại vốn Nhà nước tại doanh 
nghiệp Nhà nước và doanh nghiệp có vốn Nhà nước. Thiết lập khung tỷ lệ sở hữu Nhà nước 
cho giai đoạn 2026–2030 và tiếp nối hệ thống pháp lý mới gồm Luật 68/2025/QH15, các 
nghị định 365/2025; 366/2025; 57/2026 về quản lý, đầu tư và cơ cấu lại vốn Nhà nước.  
— Phân chia thành 3 nhóm tỷ lệ sở hữu chính: Quyết định 40 phân loại chi tiết tỷ 
lệ vốn Nhà nước cần nắm giữ theo từng ngành, lĩnh vực – trước đây các quy định cũ như: 
QĐ 22/2021, QĐ 13/2025... chưa quy định rõ ràng, khiến nhiều kế hoạch thoái vốn trước 
đây bị đình trệ vì thiếu căn cứ:

Nhóm Tỷ lệ NN nắm giữ Ngành/lĩnh vực tiêu biểu

— Với các doanh nghiệp nằm ngoài 3 nhóm trên, nguyên tắc mặc định là Nhà nước 
không tiếp tục nắm giữ vốn. Đây là căn cứ pháp lý để đưa ra lộ trình cụ thể cho hoạt động 
thoái vốn diện rộng trên toàn thị trường, đồng thời cũng là công cụ giải quyết bài toán tỷ lệ 
free-float thấp tại nhiều công ty đại chúng gốc DNNN hiện chưa đáp ứng điều kiện niêm 
yết/đăng ký giao dịch phải đối mặt với rủi ro hủy niêm yết khi không bảo đảm về cơ cấu cổ 
đông.  
— Thay đổi cơ quan giám sát: Nếu trước đây phải trình từng đề án lên Thủ tướng, 
thì nay việc phê duyệt kế hoạch cơ cấu giai đoạn 2026–2030 sẽ do Bộ/UBND tỉnh (với vai 
trò cơ quan đại diện chủ sở hữu) trực tiếp phê duyệt. Thủ tướng chỉ can thiệp phê duyệt 
đối với các doanh nghiệp đặc thù hoặc trường hợp đi chệch tiêu chí chung. Chúng tôi cho 
rằng việc giao quyền chỉ đạo xuống cấp địa phương giúp (1) rút gọn đáng kể về mặt hành 
chính; và (2) tăng cường trách nhiệm, chủ động và tính giám sát của cấp có thẩm quyền 
đối với các doanh nghiệp sở tại.  
— Lộ trình triển khai: Trong nội dung của quyết định, các đơn vị liên quan sẽ triển

Quan điểm của KBSV 
— Chúng tôi cho rằng tác động cụ thể lên thị trường chung từ Nghị quyết 40 là 
không lớn, mà tác động phần nhiều lên các doanh nghiệp đơn lẻ liên quan. Nguyên nhân 
là do phần lớn các doanh nghiệp đã công bố tỷ lệ thoái cao (hoặc toàn bộ) là nhóm doanh 
nghiệp vốn hóa vừa và nhỏ với mức độ tác động lên thị trường chung là khá yếu. Hơn thế 
nữa, nếu phương án thoái vốn có bao gồm phát hành thêm hoặc bán cổ phiếu quỹ, tác 
động thực tế sẽ nhỏ hơn đáng kể. Ở nhóm các doanh nghiệp lớn như GVR, GAS, BSR..., lộ 
trình, phương án và tỷ lệ thoái vốn còn cần chờ thêm thông tin chi tiết để đánh giá.  
— Theo Quyết định 40/2026 (áp dụng với nhóm tại Phụ lục III Nghị định 57/2026), 
khoảng 20 doanh nghiệp lớn cùng 3 ngân hàng gốc quốc doanh phải chờ Thủ tướng phê 
duyệt kế hoạch – ước tính sớm nhất tháng 08/2026 dựa trên quy trình 75 ngày làm việc 
theo Công điện 52/CĐ-TTg . Trong khi phần lớn doanh nghiệp niêm yết còn lại, như ACV, 
VNM, DPM... (nếu không thuộc Phụ lục III), được cơ quan chủ sở hữu phê duyệt trực tiếp 
trong 30 ngày làm việc. Trong đó, chúng tôi ưu tiên theo dõi các mã có chênh lệch lớn so 
với ngưỡng quy định: ACV, VNM,... (nhóm phê duyệt nhanh hơn) và HVN, BID, VCB,... (thuộc 
nhóm chờ Thủ tướng phê duyệt). 
— Các doanh nghiệp thuộc diện giảm tỷ lệ sở hữu Nhà nước có thể thực hiện thông 
qua: (1) đấu giá công khai, bao gồm bán theo lô; (2) chào bán cạnh tranh hoặc thỏa thuận 
trực tiếp theo quy định trong trường hợp đấu giá không thành công; và (3) phát hành thêm 
cổ phần để pha loãng hoặc bán cổ phiếu quỹ. Ngoài ra, nghị định 57/2026 cũng cho phép 
điều chỉnh giảm giá khởi điểm tối đa 10% mỗi lần, không quá 3 lần nhằm tăng khả năng 
thực hiện giao dịch.

Document 279: Rates Spark_ It’s about bigger buybacks, not how its financed

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rates Spark_ It’s about bigger buybacks, not how its financed.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Article | 24 August 2026 1
THINK economic and financial analysis
Article  |  24 August 2026   RATES SPARK
Rates Spark: It’s about bigger buybacks,
not how its financed
Deployment of the Treasury General Account as a financing mechanism
for buybacks is an option. But really, it s no different to structural
financing through bills, especially as the buffer is re-built. It s all about
timing. That apart, eurozone growth may not grab headlines, but is

beating consensus, adding to the upward pressure on euro rates
The best way to view effect of the buyback is by a narrowing of the swap spreads, which has already
happened
The Treasury buyback story gets ballooned by talk of TGA
deployment
We opined on talk that the US Treasury could deploy its deposit balances to finance buybacks
of Treasuries here. We make the basic point that deployment of financing from the Treasury
General Account (TGA) does not have a material effect on the potential for buybacks. They

could just as easily be financed through bills issuance. The key messaging here is not on how
the buybacks are financed, but on the threat that the buybacks could be increased again, and
again, in the future should the US Treasury deem it a suitable policy. It kicks off from
9 September, and is liable to be more than double to begin with in any case, as already
intimated by the Treasury Secretary.

### Page 2

THINK economic and financial analysis
Article | 24 August 2026 2
Has the policy been effective? The absolute level of Treasury yields is of course relevant, but is
only part of the story. The best way to view the effect of the buyback policy is through swap
spreads. As Treasury yields fall relative to SOFR rates, the swap spread narrows, and that is
what has happened. The 30yr swap spread tightened by 4bp when the doubling of long-end
buybacks was announced. And narrowed by a further 3bp through Monday. In our opinion, this

outcome is not because of talk of financing buybacks through the TGA. It's because such talk
gives the clear impression that the buyback programme could be further expanded in the
months ahead.
Improving eurozone growth adds to upward rates pressure
US headlines continue to grab markets’ attention, but the underlying growth story in the
eurozone should not be ignored, albeit the signals are more subtle. The eurozone’s economic
data have consistently beat consensus expectations over the past few months. In fact, the last

time economic surprises were this much tilted into positive territory was in 2023. Of course,
one could argue that expectations are also more subdued given the ongoing geopolitical
turmoil and the consequential higher energy costs. But still, even in absolute terms, data such
as PMIs are mostly well in positive growth territory.
An improving growth picture combined with still very positive market sentiment means more
upside risk for euro rates. Even the release of Germany’s Ifo survey outcomes on Tuesday

could contribute to the drift higher. A further improvement in the German economic outlook
would give confidence to the European Central Bank regarding a potential rate hike in
September. So unless oil and gas prices make a significant turn lower, euro rates are unlikely to
ease for now.
Tuesday’s events and market view
In terms of data, EUR rates will mainly focus on Germany’s Ifo business sentiment
indicator. Last week’s flash PMI had seen the services sector disappoint, but it saw a

surprisingly strong reading for manufacturing.
In the US, we will be getting the Conference Board’s consumer confidence index for
August, along with the July new home sales and building permit numbers. The Fed’s
Barkin will be speaking on two occasions.
In the government bond primary markets, Germany will auction €5bn in 2y bonds
while the UK sells £4bn in 7y gilts. The US auctions US$69bn of new 2y notes.
The broader Sovereign, Supranational and Agency (SSA) market also remains active.

We have seen mandates from German Land Berlin (10Y) and French Ile de France
Mobilites (10y EuGB) at the start of the week. From the Supras, the World Bank

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Bank Indonesia (BI) (19 August)

We expect Bank Indonesia to leave the BI 
rate at 5.75% in its August meeting. This 
meeting carries added significance as it 
will be chaired by acting Governor Destry 
Damayanti, after Perry Warjiyo resigned 
last month. President Prabowo has since 
nominated Deputy Governor Destry as 
the sole candidate for the position of the 
next Governor . If confirmed, the 
appointment will underscore the 
government's preference for a 
technocratic and market -oriented 
leadership transition, placing a seasoned 
BI official at the helm of the institution.  
The August rate review will provide a 
glimpse into the new leader’s priorities 
and communication style.

July inflation had slowed to 2.9% yoy 
from 3.3% in June, while the 2Q growth 
was relatively resilient at 5.3%, with 
domestic drivers in the lead, especially 
investment, followed by consumption, 
while net trade was a drag.  A firm 2Q 
growth report and stability in the rupiah 
makes the case for the BI to maintain its 
rates this month.

People Bank of China  (PBOC) (20 
August)

We expect the PBOC to keep the 1Y LPR 
unchanged next week, as resilient 
exports and stable industrial activity 
should continue to support growth 
momentum. Industrial production is 
expected to edge up from 5.3% yoy in 
June to 5.4% in July, supported by firm  
external demand. Exports stayed strong, 
rising 23.9% yoy in July, driven by the 
regional AI -electronics upcycle, while 
the hi -tech and equipment 
manufacturing sub -PMIs stayed in 
expansionary territory.

Domestic momentum, however, remains 
uneven. Retail sales growth is expected 
to moderate to 0.4% yoy in July, partly 
reflecting a high base from earlier trade-
in subsidies. Household sentiment is 
weak amid uncertain job prospects, 
slower income growth and e levated 
precautionary savings, while subdued 
property prices continue to weigh on 
household wealth and consumption. 
Investment also stays weak, with fixed 
asset investment expected to contract 
further by 6.0% yoy ytd in July. Corporate 
investment is cautio us amid the anti -
involution campaign, while the property 
sector continues to be a major drag.  
Against this backdrop, policymakers are 
likely to maintain a measured policy 
stance, relying on targeted fiscal 
support to sustain growth rather than 
further rate cuts.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead August 14, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR

Headline CPI is expected to remain at 2.0% 
yoy in July , with elevated energy prices 
offset by the subdued goods and food 
prices. Electricity fuel charges and 
petrol prices increased in July, adding to 
utilities and transport costs, while 
housing inflation should edge higher 
amid a firmer residential rental market. 
However, subdued durable goods and 
food prices, alongside a slowdown in 
mainland tourist arrivals from 10.4% yoy 
in June to 2.7% in July, should partly 
offset these upward pressures.

Japan

2Q GDP, July trade, and CPI data are 
forthcoming. GDP growth is expected to 
post an on-trend rate of 0.8% QoQ saar, 
albeit lower than the 1.8% recorded in 1Q. 
Growth is expected to be largely driven 
by domestic demand. Consumption 
indicators appeared to ha ve 
strengthened in 2Q, supported by decent 
wage increases and energy subsidies 
aimed at containing inflation. In contrast, 
exports in real terms have yet to show a 
substantial recovery in 2Q.

July trade data are expected to show 
export value growth picking up further to 
20% yoy. CPI inflation, meanwhile, is 
expected to rise to 2.0% yoy in July from 
1.7% in June. These readings would be 
consistent with the preliminary trade 
data for the first 20 days of July and the 
Tokyo CPI data released earlier. Overall, 
the data will likely strengthen the case 
for an early rate hike by the Bank of 
Japan at its September meeting. 
However, an actual move would still 
hinge on market dynamics, including 
USD/JPY movements and the outcome of 
the September FOMC meeting.

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s goods exports to 
expand by a strong 40.0% yoy in July 
2026, extending the robust 45.4% yoy 
growth in June. Exports performance 
should remain underpinned by strong 
artificial intelligence demand for 
electrical & electronics products, 
alongside resilient oil and gas shipments, 
supported by energy prices amid 
evolving tensions in Middle East. We see 
Malaysia’s headline inflation holding at 
1.9% yoy in July 2026, stable relative to 
June. Price pressures should remain in 
check, as higher fue l inflation and 
energy cost pass -through were 
mitigated by fiscal subsidies.

Singapore

We forecast Singapore’s non -oil 
domestic exports to rise by 26.5%yoy in 
July. The expansion should continue to 
be led by electronics shipments, 
supported by robust AI-related demand 
for memory chips and server -related 
products. Non -electronics exports may 
have rebounded due to favourable base 
effects, although petrochemicals 
shipments remained weak due to 
disruptions in the Strait of Hormuz.

### Page 4

The Week Ahead August 14, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         4

Taiwan

July export orders are expected to 
moderate slightly to 56.6% yoy, from 
59.4% in June. Earlier -released export 
data showed growth decelerating to 
32.9% in July, the lowest reading in five 
months. In real terms, export growth 
slowed more notably to 13.1%. This 
reinforces our view that AI -driven super 
growth has peaked an d is transitioning 
toward more normal growth in 2H. We 
expect a similar trend to be reflected in 
export orders.
Thailand

We expect Thailand’s real GDP growth to 
decelerate to 1.9% yoy in 2Q26 from a 
strong 2.8% yoy in 1Q26. This slowdown 
reflected softer private consumption 
and weaker foreign tourist arrivals, due 
to challenges arising from the conflict in 
the Middle East. G overnment spending 
and robust private investment provided 
support, alongside strong goods exports 
expansion driven by global artificial 
intelligence tailwinds. However, robust 
goods imports momentum likely 
resulted in a negative net trade 
contribution in 2Q26.

Alphabet 旗下 Waymo 宣布进军欧盟市场，计划 2027 年底前在德国慕尼⿊推出⽆⼈驾驶⽹
约⻋服务。公司将 " 数周内 " 启动⼈⼯驾驶采集数据，分阶段推进商业落地。⽬前 Waymo 每
周在美完成逾 50 万次付费出⾏，并已在德、法、荷、西四国完成注册，全球扩张提速明
显。
摘要：
Waymo 正将其⾃动驾驶出⾏版图延伸⾄欧洲。这家 Alphabet 旗下公司宣布计划于 2027 年底前在
德国慕尼⿊提供⽆⼈驾驶⽹约⻋服务，成为其进军欧盟市场的⾸次尝试，也是其第三个国际市
场。
Waymo 在周⼆的声明中表⽰，公司将 " 在未来数周内 " 派遣⻋辆在慕尼⿊街头进⾏⼈⼯驾驶测
试，⽤于构建⾼精度地图，并验证其⾃动驾驶软件能否适应当地 " 独特的驾驶环境 " 。
在向公众开放服务之前，公司将安排经过培训的⾃动驾驶专家坐于⽅向盘后进⾏监督测试。
这⼀扩张计划强化了 Waymo 在全球 Robotaxi 市场谋求主导地位的战略意图。⽬前该公司每周在
美国 11 座城市完成逾 50 万次付费出⾏，⽽慕尼⿊的布局则标志着其国际化进程进⼊欧洲核⼼市

场。
⾕歌周⼆美股盘中下跌 0.44% 。
⾸批⻋队规模有限，测试先⾏
据 Waymo 发⾔⼈透露，公司在慕尼⿊初期将部署⼩规模的全电动捷豹 I-PACE ⻋队，后续将逐步
扩⼤规模。
按照计划，慕尼⿊的测试阶段将经历两个步骤：⾸先通过⼈⼯驾驶采集数据、绘制⾼精度地
图，验证⾃动驾驶算法对当地路况的适应性；随后进⼊配备专职⼈员监督的⾃动驾驶验证阶
段。公司预计在 2027 年底前向普通公众开放商业服务。
Waymo 联席⾸席执⾏官 Tekedra Mawakana 在声明中表⽰，" 慕尼⿊是全球顶尖的出⾏与⼯程中
枢，进⼊这座城市是我们全球扩张进程中的重要⾥程碑。 "

Document 324: 「日銀の利上げで円高になる」は本当か？

Source PDF: all report pdf/「日銀の利上げで円高になる」は本当か？.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-08-06
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 8 月 6 日(木)

1/3 
「日銀の利上げで円高になる」は本当か？ 
経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・ここへ来て日銀が９月にも利上げに踏み切るとの観測が盛り上がっている（筆者は10月を予
想）。日銀は為替安定を通じて、基調的な物価上昇率が２％を上振れていかないように緩和度合
いを調節する、という予想・期待が強くなっている。では、日銀の利上げによって円安傾向が終
わるかといえば、それは難しいと考える。一方で筆者は163円超えて円安が進行するとも予想して
いない。

・理由は主に２つ。ひとつは、日米協調介入によって、大きな壁が163円近辺に作られたことがあ
る。政府対投機筋という負けられない闘いに、米政府が参戦したことの「精神的」な意味合いは
大きい。参戦した以上、米政府は円安抑制に失敗したという黒歴史を作りたくないだろう。そし
てもうひとつは、米金利低下によってドル安主導の円高が進むと考えていることがある。米国は
原油高によってヘッドライン・インフレ率こそ高まっているものの、エネルギーを除いたコア物
価は落ち着いており、その背景にある賃金上昇率も安定していることから、現在織り込まれてい
る利上げ観測は徐々に後退していくと考えられる。筆者は引き続きＦＦ金利が年内は据え置かれ
ると予想しており、来年には利下げの環境が整うとみている。そうした環境が現実のものになれ
ば、米金利は低下し、ドルは弱含むだろう。なお、ＦＦ金利先物が利上げを常に織り込んでいる
のは、ウォーシュ議長が多くを語らない姿勢を維持しているため、唐突な利上げに対する保険的
な動きがあるとみられる。 
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を10 月に 1.25％とした後、27 年末までに2.0％とするだろう。 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は3.75％で据え置くだろう。

### Page 2

2/3 
・日銀の利上げが円高に結びつかないと考えるのは、ドル円と日米金利差の説明力が落ちている一
方、米10年金利単独との相関が依然として強いことが一つである。日米金利差は2025年以降に主
として円金利上昇によって１％pt以上縮小したものの、これまでのところ円高方向への動きは観
察されていない。このことは、日本の金利上昇が為替動向に重要な変化をもたらさなかったこと
を意味する。

・もうひとつ重要な事実は、金融政策が中立的な韓国においても通貨安に歯止めがかかっていない
ことがある。円安を巡っては、日銀の緩和的な金融政策が槍玉にあげられることが多い。しかし
ながら韓国の事例を踏まえると、「日銀説」には疑問が生じる。韓国の政策金利は2.75％であ
り、同国の消費者物価上昇率と比較すると引き締め寄りの中立であり、実質金利（政策金利－消
費者物価上昇率）はプラス圏で推移している。ここで米韓金利差とウォン相場に目を向けると、
やはり説明力が落ちている。円と同様に、ウォンも米10年金利単独との相関はさほど崩れていな
い。これら簡潔なデータから言えることは、円もウォンも米金利の変動が主要な要素であるとい
うことであろう。米国の金融政策については、年初の段階で２回の利下げが見込まれていたもの
が、原油高を受けて１回超の利上げが織り込まれるに至った。そうした過程でドルが買われるこ
とに違和感はない。

仮に９月に利上げを実施しても、為替相場が転換するには米金利の低下を待つ必要があるように
思える。

Document 364: グローバル経済・金利ウォッチ

Source PDF: all report pdf/グローバル経済・金利ウォッチ.pdf
Report source: Sony FG

### Page 1

KEY

2026/6/26グローバル経済・金利ウォッチ
出所：Bloomberg
長短金利スプレッドは再び拡大へ

10年・2年債利回りスプレッド（左軸）
40年・10年債利回りスプレッド（右軸）
（年/月）
（％PT） （％PT）
1. 10年金利の動向と見通し
日本の長期金利（10年債利回り）は、高水準で推移している 。
中東情勢や日銀政策姿勢 、高市政権の財政政策により変
動が大きい展開となったものの、 2.8％を超えるような大幅上
昇には至っていない 。前回レポート で述べたように 、債券市
場のターミナルレート想定値が2 ％を超え、ここからのさらな
る上昇は物価目標を明確に超える政策金利水準の引き上
げとなるが、現段階では想定しづらいことが要因だ 。中東情
勢がエスカレーションしなかったこと 、高市政権の補正予算

規模が想定ほど拡張的ではなかったことも一因とみられる。
今月の注目点は、 6月日銀会合を受けた債券市場の動きで
あったが、総じてみて会合は無風通過となり 、特に状況に大
きな変化はみられない。債券市場のビハインドザカーブ懸念
の代理指標である長短金利スプレッドをみると （図表1）、会
合後は小幅に拡大した。内田副総裁会見では今後の利上げ
ペースに関する追加的な示唆には乏しかったこともあり 、債
券市場の今後の利上げ織り込みに大きな変化はなく 、市場
でのビハインドザカーブ懸念は後退しなかった 。そのため、
今後何らかのきっかけで 、同懸念が要因となって再び長短
スプレッドが拡大するリスクは残ったままだ。
長短金利スプレッドをやや長い目でみると、スプレッドの拡大
傾向が強まった時期は昨年 10月頃からであり 、高市自民党
総裁誕生による政府の経済政策の大幅転換が債券市場で
意識されているようだ。責任ある積極財政の方針を受け 、イ
ンフレ期待や国債需給等が材料視されているとみられる。
昨年10月以降の長期金利を①10年物BEI＋実質金利、②10

年物ASW（10年債利回り－ 10年物OIS、名目タームプレミア
ムに近似)の2軸から評価すると 、まず①については 、BEIに
よる押し上げが 50bp、実質金利が50bp であり、インフレ期待
の寄与がおよそ半分 だ。実質金利には実質タームプレミア
ムも含まれるが、正確な判別はできない。そこで、②をみると、
ASWは概ね横ばい圏で推移している 。よって、実質金利の
上昇を財政懸念によるタームプレミアムの押し上げだけで説
明するのは乱暴だろう 。高市政権の積極財政による成長期
待の高まりや日銀のストック効果縮小など、複数の要因が重
なっていると考えるのが自然だ。
BEIから算出されるインフレ期待は流動性の低さなど様々な
要因から実勢より過小評価されているとの指摘（ 例えばThe
Economistなど）が多い。結論として 、長期金利上昇の主因
はやはりインフレ期待の上昇と言えるのではないか。
2026年6月円金利見通し：ビハインドザカーブ懸念と40年・30年ゾーンの逆イールドについて
 金利の現状：長期金利は2.7％前後で推移。既に債券市場のターミナル想定値は相応に高く、高市政権の

補正予算が懸念ほど拡張的ではなかったことが要因。一方、超長期金利は発行減などから低下傾向。
 今後の見通し：長期金利はビハインドザカーブ懸念から引き続き 2.7％前後の下値の堅い展開が続くと予想。
財政政策動向次第では2.8％越えも。超長期金利は引き続き緩やかな低下傾向での推移を想定。
 40年・30年ゾーンの逆イールド：過去3年40年・30年債利回りスプレッドの急激な縮小が5月に発生しており、
毎年6月のPD懇に向けた発行減額観測が、特に40年債利回りを押し下げているとみられる。
1
issue date 2026/6/26

5年先5年物インフレスワップ（左軸）
債券市場想定の中立金利と
ターミナルレートのスプレッド（右軸）
（％） （％PT）
10年債・2年債利回りスプレッド（右軸）
今後のインフレ期待をみるうえで 、インフレスワップをやや仔
細にみると、実は5年先5年物が、5月中旬に一段切り上がっ

ている。債券市場では足下よりも中期的なインフレ期待が高
まっているということだ。 そのため、6月中旬以降に中東情勢
改善期待が高まり 、原油高が一服している中でも 、10年物
BEIならびに長期金利が大きく低下に転じる兆しは乏しい。
中長期のインフレ期待が一段上昇した時期に 、債券市場の
想定する中立金利とターミナルレートの乖離が一段と大きく
なった（図表2）。つまり日銀ビハインドザカーブ懸念 （ターミナ
ルレート想定値の上昇以上に中立金利想定値が上昇）によっ
て中長期インフレ期待が押し上げられたことがわかる。
同時期に一段とビハインドザカーブ懸念が高まった理由を一
つに特定することは困難だが、 為替介入後のドル円レートが
再び円安となったことも関係しているかもしれない 。いずれに
せよ、今後インフレ期待が抑制されて長期金利上昇に歯止め
がかかるかどうかは 、日銀の物価目標達成に向けた市場の
信認が得られるかどうか にかかっていると言える 。弊社井上
エグゼクティブエコノミストのレポート で指摘されているように 、

6月会合の結果では今後の利上げペースが加速するかどう
かは判然としなかった 。そのため、債券市場では当面 、日銀
のビハインドザカーブ懸念が燻り 、これが長期金利を押し上
げ、2.7％前後の下値の堅い展開が続くと予想される。
ただし、7月の注目はむしろ財政政策の動向だ。7月には骨太
の方針が公表される見込みで 、すでに戦略17分野への官民
合計370兆円（2040年度まで）の投資戦略が策定されると報じ
られる。財源の議論の内容次第では 、財政リスクが意識され
て長期金利は2.8％越えを試す展開となろう 。加えて、FRBの
利上げ観測の高まりにより円安地合いが強まり 、日銀の早期
利上げ観測も高まるかもしれない 。既に一部の識者からは9
～10月会合の利上げ可能性が指摘される 。当社のハウス
ビューでは、日銀6月会合の内容などを踏まえ 、日銀は利上
げ後の金融環境やインフレ動向の見極めに時間をかけると
予想し、次回利上げ時期の予想は12月のまま不変だ。
2. 40年金利の動向と見通し
40年金利は、10年金利の上昇が押し上げ要因となるものの、

前回レポート時点 （5月14日）から明確に低下し 、超長期・長
期金利スプレッドも縮小傾向 にある（前頁図表1）。以前のレ
ポートで指摘した ①外国人などの一部投資家による買い需
要、②日銀の国債買入れ比率上昇による需給悪化懸念後退、
③会計ルール緩和期待、④株高によるリバランス需要などの
要因が挙げられる。高市政権の補正予算規模が懸念ほど拡
張的ではなかった点や、 日銀の6 月会合で国債買入れ減額
停止が決まった点（ 弊社井上エグゼクティブエコノミストのレ
ポートをご参照）も、追い風となったと推察される。
先行きも、財務省の発行減など政府・ 日銀による超長期市場
安定化策への期待から、超長期金利は緩やかな低下傾向で
推移すると予想 している。ただし、買い手不足による需給軟
調や高市政権の積極財政に対する懸念、 米利上げなどによ
る海外の超長期金利上昇への警戒感などを受け 、3.8％前後
の高水準圏での推移を予想している。
注目されるのは40年・30年債利回りの逆イールドだ（図表3）。
超長期ゾーンの中でも 、40年債の相対的なアウトパフォーム

が目立つ展開となっている。
逆イールドの要因は季節性ではないか。マイナス金利解除以
降の40年・30年金利スプレッドをみると、毎年5月になるとスプ
レッドが大幅に縮小し、それが7月頭ぐらいまで続く。3年連続
同じ事象が発生しているということは、 季節性があるとみてよ
いだろう。背景には、毎年6月に行われるPD懇に向けて、5月
頃から超長期国債の発行減額観測が高まっていたことがあ
る。このうち、40年債は他の年限と比べて発行減額が進んで
いる（今年の40年債発行予定額は 30年債の4分の1にとどま
る）こともあり 、需給ひっ迫 （国債発行減） 観測の影響を相対
的に受けやすいとみられる 。需要面から見れば、報道では昨
年の円金利高騰を受けた投資抑制が一巡し 、一部の生保の
超長期債に投資する動きが出始めているようだ 。その影響が
加わったことで 、今年6月に逆イールドが発生したとみられる 。
特に、超長期ゾーンについては日銀が買入れオペ額を減額
させず維持していたこともあり 、今年の発行減額観測はより

40年債の利回りを押し下げたと推察される 。先行きも、発行
減額期待が続けば逆イールドは当面続きそうだが、 毎年7月
になるとスプレッドは再び拡大している 。おそらく骨太の方針
などを受けた財政拡張観測が高まりやすい季節性があるの
だろう。前述したように 、今年の骨太方針と国債発行計画へ
の影響は大変な注目を集めており、財政リスクが高まれば逆
イールドが解消する可能性もある 。例年のパターン通りであ
れば、スプレッドはゼロ近傍で推移するのではないか。
なお、この40年・30年の逆イールドの発生は特別な現象と捉
えるべきではない 。約10年前にも逆イールドが発生したこと
がある点や、足元の海外金利（40年債利回りを発行している
ヨーロッパ諸国 ）をみても逆イールドは発生している 。グロー
バルでも40年債が選好されて利回りが低下していることが 、
日本の40年債利回り低下の一因だろう。背景には株高による
リバランス需要や長寿化対応があるのかもしれない。ただし、
米国は40 年債利回りを発行していないため 、上記要因は逆

イールドの追加的な材料にすぎないだろう。
2

Document 365: タイGDP（26年4-6月期）～輸入急増で成長鈍化も、輸出・設備投資は堅調

Source PDF: all report pdf/タイGDP（26年4-6月期）～輸入急増で成長鈍化も、輸出・設備投資は堅調.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

【結果概要】 
タイの2026 年4-6 月期の実質GDP 成長率1は前年同期比1.9％となり、 前期の同2.8％から減速し
た（図表１）。市場予想2（1.8％） を小幅に上回ったものの、 前年同期比では3 四半期ぶりに伸びが
鈍化した。 また、 季節調整済み前期比成長率は▲0.2％と前期 （0.6％） からマイナスに転じており、
景気回復の勢いが弱まった。 
需要項目別にみると、輸出が高い伸びを維持し、民間投資も機械・設備投資を中心に加速した一
方、民間消費や政府消費が鈍化し、公共投資は減少に転じた。また、輸入が輸出を大きく上回って
増加したため、純輸出の成長率寄与度はマイナス幅が拡大した。 
NESDC は 2026 年通年の成長率見通しを従来の1.5～2.5％から 2.0～2.5％へ上方修正した。今後
は、輸出・投資の好調が内需に波及するかが焦点となる。

【支出別の詳細】 
民間消費は前年同期比 1.9％となり、前期（3.3％）から鈍化した（図表２） 。内訳をみると、半
耐久財は 3.9％と前期（3.6％）から加速した。一方、非耐久財は2.4％、耐久財は 3.8％、サービ
ス消費は 0.8％といずれも鈍化した。自動車購入や宿泊・飲食、運輸サービスへの支出の伸びが低

1  8 月 17 日、タイの国家経済社会開発委員会（ＮＥＳＤＣ）が 2026 年 4-6 月期の国内総生産（ＧＤＰ）を公表した。 
2  Bloomberg 調査

タイ GDP（26 年 4-6 月期） 
～輸入急増で成長鈍化も、輸出・設備投資は堅調

経済研究部 准主任研究員 斉藤 誠 
(03)3512-1780  msaitou@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

【産業別の詳細】 
産業別にみると、農林水産業、鉱工業、サービス業はいずれも前期から減速した。 
農林水産業は前年同期比 1.5％となり、前期（2.0％）から鈍化した（図表３） 。果物やゴム、豚
などの生産が増加した一方、 高温や干ばつの影響を受けてコメやアブラヤシが減産となり、 漁業も
減少した。 
鉱工業は前年同期比 1.1％となり、前期（1.8％）から鈍化した。鉱業は 5.1％と前期（8.9％）
から伸びが低下したほか、 製造業は0.1％と前期 （1.0％） から減速した。 製造業ではコンピュータ
ー・電子部品の生産は引き続き堅調だったものの増勢が鈍化し、自動車生産も減少した。一方、電
気・ガス業は6.2％と前期（4.3％）から加速した。 
サービス業は前年同期比2.4％となり、前期（3.5％）から減速した。宿泊・飲食業は1.5％、運
輸・倉庫業は 2.6％といずれも伸びが鈍化し、外国人観光客数の減少や国内旅行の鈍化が重石とな
った。小売・卸売業も4.5％と前期（5.8％）から鈍化した。また、建設業は0.1％と前期（6.2％）
（図表３）

### Page 3

【景気の見方と今後の注目点】 
4-6 月期のタイ経済は前年同期比 1.9％と前期（2.8％）から減速し、季節調整済み前期比でも
▲0.2％とマイナスに転じた。輸入の増加が輸出を大きく上回り、純輸出の下押しが強まったこと
が成長率を押し下げた。もっとも、輸入増の一部は好調な輸出向け生産や設備投資に伴う中間財・
資本財の需要増を反映しており、景気の内容は成長率ほど弱くない。一方、民間消費が鈍化したほ
か、建設業が大幅に減速し、製造業や観光関連業種の伸びも低下しており、景気回復の裾野はなお
広がりを欠いている。 
先行きの焦点は、 好調な輸出と民間投資が国内需要にどこまで波及するかである。NESDC は2026
年の成長率見通しを従来の 1.5～2.5％から 2.0～2.5％へ上方修正した。世界経済・貿易の想定引
き上げや電子機器などへの需要拡大を背景に、 財輸出額の伸びを9.6％から15.1％へ引き上げたほ
か、機械・設備投資の拡大などを踏まえ、民間投資も 3.7％から 9.6％へ大幅に上方修正した。輸
出と民間投資が引き続き景気を支えるとみられるなか、 こうした好調さが国内生産や雇用、 所得の
改善につながるかが注目される。 
内需では、民間消費が持ち直すかが注目される。消費者信頼感は低下しており、高水準の家計債
務も引き続き消費の重石となる。もっとも、NESDC はエネルギー価格の想定引き下げに伴うインフ
レ圧力の緩和や所得水準の上昇を踏まえ、2026 年の民間消費の伸びを2.4％から 2.6％へ小幅に上
方修正した。また、外国人観光客数は前年を下回る状況が続いているが、一人当たり旅行支出の増
加を背景に、 外国人観光収入見通しは1.51 兆バーツから1.65 兆バーツへ引き上げられており、 観
光収入の回復がサービス業を下支えするかも注目される。 
リスク面では、 農業への下押し圧力が強まっている点に注意が必要である。 米国の通商政策や中
東情勢に伴うエネルギー価格上昇の影響は、 輸出見通しの上方修正やエネルギー価格想定の引き下
げを踏まえると、足元ではやや後退している。一方、農業では高温や干ばつの影響が顕在化してお
り、エルニーニョに伴う水不足が今後の生産や農家所得を下押しする可能性がある。

本資料記載のデータは各種の情報源から入手・加工したものであり、その正確性と完全性を保証するものではありません。 
また、本資料は情報提供が目的であり、記載の意見や予測は、いかなる契約の締結や解約を勧誘するものではありません。

（資料）中国国家統計局、CEICより、ニッセイ基礎研究所作成
1. 中国の 2026 年 4～6 月期の実質 GDP 成長率は、前年同期比＋4.3％と、前期（26 年 1～3 月
期）の同＋5.0％から減速した（下左図）。AI 投資ブームなどを背景に外需の好調が続いた一
方、中東情勢の不安定化による原油価格上昇や、国内の不動産不況および地方債務問題の影
響により内需が悪化した。7 月には異常気象による天候要因の下押し圧力も重なった。 政府に
よる経済対策は実施されているものの、経済全体を好転させるほどの効果は表れていない。 
2. 今後を見通すと、内需に関しては、中東情勢の影響による下押し圧力が今しばらく続く見込
みだ。足元の原油価格は 4～6 月期に比べると低下し、企業物価上昇は頭打ちとなっている
が、石油化学産業を中心に企業収益への影響が今後顕在化する可能性があり注視が必要だ。
不動産を中心に悪化する投資は、25 年後半の落ち込みの反動で持ち直すと見込まれるが、下
押し圧力は残るだろう。7 月 30 日開催の中央政治局会議では、不動産や地方債務問題につい
て目新しい考えは示されなかった。追加経済対策を検討する方針は示されたものの、当面は
様子見が続くと予想される。 
3. 以上を踏まえ、26 年から 27 年にかけての実質 GDP 成長率は、 それぞれ＋4.4％、 ＋3.8％と、
減速が続くとみている（下右図） 。好調な外需と弱い内需という現在の不安定な構図は変わら
ないだろう。26 年は「＋4.5～5.0％」の成長率目標に届かない可能性が依然として残る。国
内外の下押し圧力が今後どの程度続くか、また、仮に目標未達の可能性が現実味を帯びた場
合、機動的に追加対策が講じられるかが当面の注目点となる。

### Page 3

投資の動向について、26 年 7 月の固定資産投資の前年同月比伸び率（名目、 以下同）は、 前月から低
下し、依然としてマイナス圏での推移を続けている（図表 7）。内訳をみると、製造業、インフラ、不動
産開発など主要な業種でいずれもマイナス幅が拡大した。設備投資は、前月から伸び率が上昇した。中
国国家統計局は、固定資産投資の悪化に関して、異常気象や厳しい外部環境の影響を指摘する一方、量
的拡大よりも質や効率の向上を重視するとの見解は前月から変わっていない。 
外需の動向について、26 年 7 月の輸出（ドル建て）の伸び率は、前月から低下した（図表 8）。国・
地域別にみると、米国向けが前年増を続けており、前月からはやや加速した。財別では、プラスチック
製品や紡績糸等、鋼材、家電、自動車、自動車部品など、主要品目の多くが減速した。衣類等やコンピ
ュータ・同部品は加速した。集積回路も減速したものの、価格高騰の影響で3 桁を超える伸びが続いて
いる。輸入（ドル建て）の伸び率は、前月から低下した。原油輸入のマイナス幅は縮小した。貿易収支
は、1,125 億ドルの黒字となり、前年同月比では増加した。

### Page 4

（消費・家計） 
消費の動向について、26 年 7 月の小売売上高の伸び率は、前月から低下した（図表 9）。財の伸び率
が低下した一方、外食サービスの伸び率は上昇した。 
一定規模以上企業を対象にした統計で財の品目別の動向をみると、衣類等の伸び率は前月のプラスか
らマイナスに転じた。化粧品は伸び率が低下、宝飾品はマイナス幅が拡大した（図表 10）。26 年も継続
されている耐久消費財の買い替え支援策の対象商品については、家電・AV 機器でマイナス幅が縮小、
通信機器では伸び率が上昇した一方、オフィス用品等は伸び率が低下、家具はマイナス幅が拡大した。
自動車、不動産関連の財（建築・内装材）もマイナス幅が拡大した。

### Page 5

（不動産市場） 
不動産市場について、26 年 7 月の住宅販売床面積の伸び率は、マイナス幅が前月に続き拡大した（図
表 13）。住宅販売価格（70 都市単純平均）の前年同月比は、22 年 4 月以降、52 カ月連続でマイナスと
なっている。26 年 7 月は、マイナス幅が前月に続き小幅に縮小した。 
供給側の動向に関して、住宅着工床面積（3 カ月後方移動平均）の伸び率は、前月からマイナス幅が
小幅に縮小した（図表 14）。住宅竣工床面積（同上）の伸び率は、マイナス幅が前月に続き拡大した。
住宅完成在庫床面積は、 伸び率が 0％まで低下し、 増加に歯止めがかかった。 また、 不動産開発資金（同
上）の伸び率は、依然として前年同月比でマイナスとなっており、マイナス幅は 6 月から小幅に縮小し
た。

### Page 6

３. 物価・金融の動向 
（物価） 
物価の動向について、26 年 7 月の消費者物価指数（CPI）の前年同月比（以下同）は、前月に続き低
下した（図表 17）。豚肉のマイナス幅が縮小した一方、自動車等燃料は大幅に低下した。食品・エネル
ギーを除くコア CPI も、前月に続き小幅に低下した。工業生産者出荷価格 （PPI） は、22 年 10 月以降、
41 カ月連続でマイナスであったが、原油価格上昇等を受け26 年 3 月にプラスに転じた。その後もプラ
ス圏で推移しているものの、7 月は前月から低下した。（図表 18）。

▎FOMC 按兵不動，但決策分歧偏鷹 
7 月 FOMC 如預期 維持 聯邦基 金利 率於 3.50% ～
3.75%區間不變，惟 3 名委員投票支持升息，顯示會內
對通膨風險與政策強度仍有分歧 。 主席華許會後淡化前
瞻指引，使市場更仰賴數據與通膨風險自行定價，長端
殖利率因而維持高位震盪。隨後公布的 6 月 PCE 與核
心 PCE 低於預期 ， 一度緩和通膨壓力 。 不過 ， 近期就業
數據仍顯示勞動市場尚未明顯轉弱 ， 市場焦點轉向非農
就業報告，PCE 降溫雖緩和短線通膨壓力，但仍遠高於
2%目標，Fed 維持利率不變的立場仍具支撐。

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/8/6 
▎中東局勢反覆，債市反彈後轉弱 
近期美伊談判消息持續牽動油價與長端利率走勢。市
場一度期待雙方重回談判桌，油價回落帶動 10 年期美
債殖利率下行，主要債券短暫反彈。不過，隨後荷莫
茲海峽航運安排仍陷僵局，市場對原油供應的疑慮再
起，油價回升並推動殖利率反彈，債市回吐前期漲
幅。整體而言，中東風險與長端利率反覆，使債市持
續承壓。

圖 2 殖利率高位波動，壓抑債市表現

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/8/6 
◼ 7 月 FOMC 利率不變，但 3 名委員支持升息，決策分歧偏鷹。PCE 雖略降溫但仍
高於目標，就業尚未明顯轉弱，長端殖利率維持高檔 波動。美伊談判進展一度帶
動油價與利率回落，但荷莫茲海峽航運安排陷僵局，油價疑慮再起，債市續承壓。 
◼ 債市今年受地緣風險干擾，但航太債表現仍相對穩定，主因產業基本面具支撐。
全球飛機訂單積壓仍高，待交付飛機規模已接近目前營運機隊的 6 成，反映航空
公司擴充機隊與汰舊換新需求延續。主要商用飛機製造商交付量預估 今年年增
8.3%，並於 2028 年前維持回升， 整機、 引擎與零組件供應鏈需求可望同步受惠。 
◼ 航太交付復甦已轉化為財務支撐 ， 主要廠商獲利改善後 ， 付息能力也自低點回升 ，
信用基本面仍有支撐。飛機整機製造商較直接受惠訂單積壓與交付回升，航太供
應鏈公司則受惠維修與備品需求，但仍需留意新引擎出貨對利潤率的壓力。配置
上可優先考慮訂單能見度較高、受惠交付復甦較直接的投 等債發行公司。

▎航空訂單積壓高，交付展望持續回升 
儘管地緣風險反覆，使債市表現持續承壓，商用飛機需
求仍受到訂單積壓與交付復甦支撐 。 近年航空旅客與貨
運需求持續恢復 ， 航空公司擴充機隊與汰舊換新需求延
續，使新機需求維持支撐。根據 IATA(國際航空運輸協
會)資料顯示 ， 截至2026 年 5 月 ， 全球飛機訂單積壓達
18,100 架，約當現役機隊近 60%，意即待交付飛機規
模已接近目前營運機隊的 6 成 ， 反映新機需求與待交付
訂單水位仍高。

圖 3 全球飛機訂單積壓處於高檔

資料來源：IATA，2026/5 月

由於商用飛機生產流程長、供應鏈分工複雜，訂單消化
仍需時間，根據 JPM 預估，主要商用飛機製造商交付
量在 2026 年可望年增 8.3%，並於 2028 年前維持回
升（圖 4）。隨交付量回升，整機、引擎與零組件供應
鏈的產能利用率與出貨動能也可望延續。

圖 4 商用飛機交付量預估回升

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/7/21 
▎空中巴士、波音主導商用飛機交付 
從主要製造商來看 ， 全球商用飛機交付主要由歐洲空中
巴士 （Airbus） 與美國波音 （Boeing） 主導，兩者長期
為大型商用飛機市場的核心供應商。其中，空中巴士交
付規模維持居前，波音則在 2024 年低基期後回升，巴
西航空工業公司 （Embraer） 雖為區域型商用飛機重要
製造商，但交付規模相對較小。

圖 5 空中巴士與波音主導商用飛機交付

資料來源：Bloomberg，2026/7/21

▎航太廠商獲利改善，供應鏈同步受惠 
隨整機交付回升， 航太獲利修復不只來自整機製造端 ，
也來自引擎、航太系統、零組件與售後維修服務。主要
航太製造與供應鏈公司的過去 12 個月 EBITDA 自 2024
年底低點回升，反映新機交付改善帶動相關出貨，而既
有機隊持續營運，也支撐引擎維修、備品更換與售後維
修服務需求。 
從產業鏈分工來看 ， 整機製造商與供應鏈公司的獲利修
復程度仍有差異 。 空中巴士*（Airbus） 交付與獲利表現
相對穩定，波音*（Boeing）則受近年安全與品質事件
影響，生產與交付恢復較慢，商用飛機獲利仍處修復階
段，但虧損幅度已有收斂。即使整機端尚未完全修復，
主要航太製造與供應鏈公司 EBITDA 仍已回升 ， 反映奇
異航太*（GE Aerospace）、雷神科技* (RTX)與霍尼韋
爾國際*（Honeywell International ）等引擎、航太系
統與零組件公司，受惠零組件出貨與維修備品需求，獲
利來源已由整機端擴散至供應鏈。 
*上述相關標的僅舉例說明，非單一標的推薦。

主要航太廠商獲利改善後，利息保障倍數自 2025 年以
來重新走升 ， 由約8 倍回升至 2026 年第 1 季約 10 倍。
雖低於 2023 年高位，但仍處疫情後相對高檔，顯示在
利率水準仍高、利息費用壓力尚未明顯消退下，主要航
太廠商的獲利改善仍足以支撐利息支出。

圖 7 利息保障倍數自低點回升

▎航太債基本面仍有支撐 
整體來看，商用飛機訂單積壓仍高、交付量預期回升，
加上主要航太製造與供應鏈公司獲利與付息能力改善，
均有助支撐航太債信用基本面。 
彭博對主要航太製造與供應鏈公司的觀點大致偏正向，
重點包括交付回升、供應鏈改善、國防需求支撐與服務
業務延續。 其中 ， 空中巴士*（Airbus） 交付與獲利表現
相對穩定，波音*（Boeing）則在 2024 年低基期後交
付 回 升 ， 但 獲 利 修 復 仍 需 時 間 。 奇 異 航 太* （ GE 
Aerospace）、RTX 公司*（RTX Corp）等供應鏈公司
仍受惠備品、維修與國防需求，但隨新機交付增加，低
利潤的新引擎出貨比重提高， 短期利潤率仍可能承壓。

Document 432: 内閣府「中長期試算」2026年７月アップデート－ＰＢは大幅改善したが、金利ある世界で利...

Source PDF: all report pdf/内閣府「中長期試算」2026年７月アップデート－ＰＢは大幅改善したが、金利ある世界で利....pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-08-05
Pages: 9
